================================================
FILE: README.md
================================================
# Saliency-guided attention on a frozen Vision Transformer under temporal drift

Final-year project. A satellite-image classifier is trained on 2010–13 images and must be updated for 2016–17,
under natural drift and cloud cover. The project asks whether the old model's own patch-level saliency can make
that update better, cheaper or easier to audit, and compares it with learned attention and LoRA.

![System overview](figures/fmow_v2/figures/fig_overview.png)

## Headline results

Macro-F1 (%) on 2016–17 fMoW test images, models trained on clean 2014–15 images
(heads: mean of 3 seeds; LoRA and hybrid: seed 42).

| Model | Trainable params | Backbone weights changed | Clean | Cloud beside object | Cloud over object | Real clouds |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Frozen baseline | 0 | 0 | 49.1 | 46.8 | 42.1 | 44.7 |
| Learned attention | 197k | 0 | 60.1 | 58.3 | 49.4 | 55.2 |
| Hard saliency gate | 197k | 0 | 59.8 | 57.4 | 48.1 | 55.7 |
| Soft saliency gate | 197k | 0 | 60.3 | 58.7 | 49.6 | 55.6 |
| Hybrid: LoRA r1 (2 blocks) + soft gate | 47k | 25 M | 60.0 | 58.7 | 49.9 | 54.8 |
| LoRA r2 (6 blocks) | 221k | 75 M | **62.7** | **60.7** | **52.0** | **60.1** |

- **Accuracy:** LoRA is the most accurate update; attention heads come within about 2.5 points without touching
  the backbone and train in about a minute.
- **Saliency** adds neither accuracy nor cloud robustness (shuffled-map controls match every saliency head).
- **What saliency does give** is explanation: both the old and the updated heads decompose *exactly* into
  per-patch contributions, and saliency-gated heads put most of their support on the object.

![Accuracy of the main models](figures/fmow_v2/figures/fig_accuracy.png)
![Explanations before and after an update](figures/fmow_v2/figures/fig_explain_update.png)

## Repository layout

| Path | Contents |
|---|---|
| `src/fyp_drift/` | Python package: data, models, training and all experiments (`evaluation/`, v2 additions in `evaluation/fmow_v2/`) |
| `models/fmow_v2/` | Trained models (safetensors) with a one-line inference CLI |
| `results/fmow_v2/` | Saved output of every v2 run, with per-image predictions |
| `figures/fmow_v2/` | Every figure, and the code + data that draw it |
| `scripts/` | Run scripts (`fmow_v2/`) and early pilots (`pilots/`) |
| `docs/` | Guides (below) and earlier protocol notes |
| `tests/` | Unit tests (`pytest`) |
| `deploy/` | Server setup and batch scripts |

The earlier CLEAR-100 version of the project (transparent router) is preserved on the `initial` branch.

## Guides

| Guide | For |
|---|---|
| [Getting started](docs/getting_started.md) | Install, download the data, run, rebuild figures |
| [Data](docs/data.md) | fMoW source, subset, splits, preparation, clouds |
| [Code map](docs/code_map.md) | What each part of the code does, with an architecture diagram |
| [Experiment run order](docs/fmow_v2.md) | Every v2 step, its command and output, protocol notes |
| [Trained models](docs/models.md) | Saved models, inference, re-exporting |

## Quick start

```bash
pip install -e . && pip install seaborn scipy safetensors
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.models predict models/fmow_v2/soft_gate my_site.jpg --map attention.png
```



================================================
FILE: bootstrap.ps1
================================================
param(
    [ValidateSet('Setup', 'Ingest', 'Baseline', 'Xai', 'Siamese', 'Evaluate', 'Drift', 'All')]
    [string]$Action = 'Setup',
    [int]$Epochs = 3,
    [int]$BatchSize = 8
)

$ErrorActionPreference = 'Stop'
Set-Location -LiteralPath $PSScriptRoot

function Get-UvPath {
    $found = Get-Command uv -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue
    if ($found) { return $found.Source }
    foreach ($candidate in @(
        (Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'tools\uv.exe'),
        (Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE '.local\bin\uv.exe'),
        (Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Microsoft\WinGet\Links\uv.exe'),
        (Join-Path $env:USERPROFILE '.cargo\bin\uv.exe')
    )) { if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $candidate) { return $candidate } }
    return $null
}

$uv = Get-UvPath
if (-not $uv) {
    $env:UV_UNMANAGED_INSTALL = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot 'tools'
    $env:UV_NO_MODIFY_PATH = '1'
    Invoke-Expression (Invoke-RestMethod -Uri 'https://astral.sh/uv/install.ps1')
    $uv = Get-UvPath
    if (-not $uv) { throw 'The project-local uv installation did not produce tools\uv.exe.' }
}

& $uv sync
if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) {
    # Some Windows uv versions create Python 3.11 but fail to follow its minor-version junction once.
    $pythonRoot = Join-Path $env:APPDATA 'uv\python'
    $installed = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $pythonRoot -Directory -Filter 'cpython-3.11.*-windows-x86_64-none' -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue |
        Sort-Object Name -Descending | Select-Object -First 1
    $pythonExe = if ($installed) { Join-Path $installed.FullName 'python.exe' } else { $null }
    if (-not $pythonExe -or -not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $pythonExe)) { throw "uv sync failed: $LASTEXITCODE" }
    & $uv sync --python $pythonExe
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "uv sync failed: $LASTEXITCODE" }
}

function Invoke-DataIngest {
    Write-Host 'Downloading CLEAR-100 image archives (about 4.6 GB total; resumable).'
    & $uv run fyp-ingest
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Dataset ingestion failed: $LASTEXITCODE" }
}
function Invoke-Train([string]$method) {
    & $uv run fyp-train --method $method --epochs $Epochs --batch-size $BatchSize
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Training failed for ${method}: $LASTEXITCODE" }
}
function Get-LatestRun([string]$method) {
    $folder = Join-Path $PSScriptRoot "runs\$method"
    if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $folder)) { throw "No runs for $method" }
    $run = Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath $folder -Directory | Sort-Object Name -Descending | Select-Object -First 1
    if (-not $run -or -not (Test-Path -LiteralPath (Join-Path $run.FullName 'models\best.pt'))) {
        throw "No best checkpoint for $method"
    }
    return $run.FullName
}
function Invoke-Eval([string]$method) {
    $run = Get-LatestRun $method
    $checkpoint = Join-Path $run 'models\best.pt'
    & $uv run fyp-evaluate --method $method --checkpoint $checkpoint --batch-size $BatchSize --output (Join-Path $run 'metrics\recheck')
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Evaluation failed for ${method}: $LASTEXITCODE" }
}
function Invoke-Drift([string]$method) {
    $run = Get-LatestRun $method
    $checkpoint = Join-Path $run 'models\best.pt'
    & $uv run fyp-evaluate-drift --method $method --checkpoint $checkpoint --batch-size $BatchSize --output (Join-Path $run 'metrics\drift')
    if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Drift evaluation failed for ${method}: $LASTEXITCODE" }
}

switch ($Action) {
    'Setup' { Write-Host 'Environment ready. Run -Action Ingest to download the benchmark.' }
    'Ingest' { Invoke-DataIngest }
    'Baseline' { Invoke-Train 'baseline' }
    'Xai' { Invoke-Train 'xai' }
    'Siamese' {
        $baseRun = Get-LatestRun 'xai'
        & $uv run fyp-train-router --base-checkpoint (Join-Path $baseRun 'models\best.pt') --epochs $Epochs --batch-size $BatchSize
        if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Siamese adaptation failed: $LASTEXITCODE" }
    }
    'Evaluate' { Invoke-Eval 'baseline'; Invoke-Eval 'xai' }
    'Drift' { Invoke-Drift 'baseline'; Invoke-Drift 'xai' }
    'All' {
        Invoke-Train 'baseline'
        Invoke-Train 'xai'
        Invoke-Eval 'baseline'
        Invoke-Eval 'xai'
        $baseRun = Get-LatestRun 'xai'
        & $uv run fyp-train-router --base-checkpoint (Join-Path $baseRun 'models\best.pt') --epochs $Epochs --batch-size $BatchSize
        if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { throw "Siamese adaptation failed: $LASTEXITCODE" }
    }
}

FILE: deploy/launch.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run ingestion and training in order while retaining separate stage logs.
set -euo pipefail

project_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
cd "$project_dir"

if [[ "${1:-}" == "--detach" ]]; then
  shift
  if ! command -v tmux >/dev/null; then
    echo "tmux is required for --detach" >&2
    exit 2
  fi
  session="fyp-$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)"
  printf -v command '%q ' bash "${project_dir}/deploy/launch.sh" "$@"
  tmux new-session -d -s "$session" "$command"
  echo "Started tmux session: $session"
  echo "Watch: tmux attach -t $session"
  echo "Logs: ${project_dir}/runs/pipelines/"
  exit 0
fi

mode="${1:-all}"
if [[ "$#" -gt 0 ]]; then shift; fi
if [[ "$mode" != "all" && "$mode" != "ingest" && "$mode" != "train" ]]; then
  echo "Usage: bash deploy/launch.sh [--detach] {all|ingest|train} [experiment options]" >&2
  exit 2
fi
if [[ "$mode" == "ingest" && "$#" -gt 0 ]]; then
  echo "Experiment options apply to all or train mode" >&2
  exit 2
fi

run_dir="${project_dir}/runs/pipelines/$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)"
mkdir -p "$run_dir"
data_dir="$(readlink -f -- "${project_dir}/data")"
if [[ ! -d "$data_dir" || ! -w "$data_dir" ]]; then
  echo "Data directory is missing or not writable: $data_dir" >&2
  exit 2
fi
record_exit() {
  local status="$?"
  printf 'exit_code=%s\nended_utc=%s\n' "$status" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" >> "${run_dir}/run.txt"
}
trap record_exit EXIT
printf 'mode=%s\ncommit=%s\nstarted_utc=%s\n' \
  "$mode" "$(git rev-parse HEAD)" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" > "${run_dir}/run.txt"
echo "Pipeline logs: $run_dir"
echo "Data directory: ${project_dir}/data -> $data_dir"

if [[ "$mode" == "all" || "$mode" == "ingest" ]]; then
  echo "Starting CLEAR-100 ingestion"
  bash deploy/server.sh ingest 2>&1 | tee "${run_dir}/ingest.log"
fi
if [[ "$mode" == "all" || "$mode" == "train" ]]; then
  echo "Starting GPU experiment"
  bash deploy/server.sh experiment "$@" 2>&1 | tee "${run_dir}/train.log"
fi
echo "Pipeline completed: $run_dir"



================================================
FILE: deploy/remote_setup.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

project_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
storage_dir="${FYP_STORAGE_DIR:-/mnt/sdd/FYP}"
data_dir="${FYP_DATA_DIR:-${storage_dir}/data}"
cache_dir="${FYP_CACHE_DIR:-${storage_dir}/cache}"
tools_dir="${FYP_TOOLS_DIR:-${storage_dir}/tools}"

if [[ ! -f "${project_dir}/pyproject.toml" || ! -f "${project_dir}/uv.lock" ]]; then
  echo "Expected the FYP checkout at ${project_dir}" >&2
  exit 1
fi
if [[ ! -w /mnt/sdd ]]; then
  echo "The mounted data volume /mnt/sdd is not writable" >&2
  exit 1
fi

mkdir -p "${data_dir}" "${storage_dir}/runs" "${cache_dir}/uv" "${tools_dir}"

link_dir() {
  local name="$1"
  local destination="$2"
  local link="${project_dir}/${name}"
  if [[ -L "$link" ]]; then
    if [[ "$(readlink -f "$link")" != "$(readlink -f "$destination")" ]]; then
      echo "Existing ${link} points elsewhere" >&2
      exit 1
    fi
  elif [[ -e "$link" ]]; then
    echo "Existing ${link} is not a symlink; refusing to replace it" >&2
    exit 1
  else
    ln -s "$destination" "$link"
  fi
}

link_dir data "${data_dir}"
link_dir runs "${storage_dir}/runs"
link_dir tools "${tools_dir}"
link_dir cache "${cache_dir}"

if [[ ! -x "${tools_dir}/uv" ]]; then
  curl -LsSf https://astral.sh/uv/install.sh | env UV_UNMANAGED_INSTALL="${tools_dir}" sh
fi

export UV_CACHE_DIR="${cache_dir}/uv"
export UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT="${storage_dir}/venv"
"${tools_dir}/uv" sync --locked --project "$project_dir"
link_dir .venv "${storage_dir}/venv"

echo "FYP ready: ${project_dir}"
echo "Persistent data: ${data_dir}"
echo "Persistent runs: ${storage_dir}/runs"



================================================
FILE: deploy/run_corrected_finetune_20261031.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Complete the corrected CLEAR comparison with a conventional uniform-pooling arm.
set -euo pipefail

project_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
cd "$project_dir"
export PYTHONPATH="${project_dir}/src"
export HF_HOME="${project_dir}/cache/huggingface"

python="${project_dir}/.venv/bin/python"
root="${project_dir}/runs/validation-20261031"
logs="${root}/logs"
mkdir -p "$logs" "$HF_HOME"

record_exit() {
  status="$?"
  printf 'finetune_exit_code=%s ended_utc=%s\n' "$status" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" >> "${logs}/finetune-progress.log"
}
trap record_exit EXIT

run_stage() {
  name="$1"
  shift
  printf 'starting=%s at=%s\n' "$name" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" | tee -a "${logs}/finetune-progress.log"
  "$@" > "${logs}/${name}.log" 2>&1
  printf 'completed=%s at=%s\n' "$name" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" | tee -a "${logs}/finetune-progress.log"
}

base="${root}/baseline/historical-uniform-2009-seed42/models/best.pt"
run_stage historical-uniform-2009-seed42 \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.training.classifier \
    --method baseline --cv-val-year 2009 --epochs 3 --batch-size 8 \
    --seed 42 --data-root data --output "$root" \
    --run-id historical-uniform-2009-seed42 --log-every 100
test -f "$base"

run_stage corrected-finetune-seed42 \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.training.fine_tune \
    --base-checkpoint "$base" --epochs 5 --batch-size 8 \
    --selection-fraction 0.5 --seed 42 --data-root data \
    --output "$root" --run-id corrected-finetune-seed42 \
    --log-every 50 --drift-scenarios \
    clean illumination white_balance sensor_noise defocus_blur motion_blur \
    jpeg_compression resolution_loss viewpoint_shift occlusion realistic_mix

#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

cd "$(dirname "$0")/.."

if [[ $# -lt 2 ]]; then
  echo "Usage: $0 <router-run-dir> <historical-checkpoint> [output-dir]" >&2
  exit 2
fi

run_dir="$1"
base_checkpoint="$2"
output_root="${3:-runs/audits/drift}"

mkdir -p "$output_root"
for scenario in motion_blur viewpoint_shift realistic_mix; do
  echo "START ${scenario}"
  tools/uv run --no-sync fyp-audit-router \
    --base-checkpoint "$base_checkpoint" \
    --run-dir "$run_dir" \
    --output "$output_root/audit-${scenario}" \
    --year 2014 \
    --scenario "$scenario" \
    --severity 3 \
    --batch-size 16 \
    --top-k 2
  echo "DONE ${scenario}"
done

FILE: deploy/run_drift_v2.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

project_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
storage_dir="$(dirname -- "$(readlink -f -- "${project_dir}/runs")")"
uv="${project_dir}/tools/uv"
epochs="${EPOCHS:-5}"
batch_size="${BATCH_SIZE:-8}"
seed="${SEED:-42}"
stamp="${RUN_STAMP:-$(date -u +%Y%m%dT%H%M%SZ)}"
base_checkpoint="${BASE_CHECKPOINT:-}"

if [[ -z "$base_checkpoint" ]]; then
  while IFS= read -r candidate; do
    config="$(dirname -- "$(dirname -- "$candidate")")/config.json"
    if [[ -f "$config" ]] && grep -q '"cv_val_year": 2009' "$config"; then
      base_checkpoint="$candidate"
    fi
  done < <(find "${project_dir}/runs/xai" -mindepth 3 -maxdepth 3 \
    -path '*/models/best.pt' -type f | sort)
fi
if [[ ! -f "$base_checkpoint" ]]; then
  echo "Historical XAI checkpoint missing: $base_checkpoint" >&2
  exit 1
fi

export UV_CACHE_DIR="$(readlink -f -- "${project_dir}/cache")/uv"
export UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT="${storage_dir}/venv"
export HF_HOME="$(readlink -f -- "${project_dir}/cache")/huggingface"
mkdir -p "$HF_HOME" "${project_dir}/runs/experiments/drift-v2-${stamp}"
log="${project_dir}/runs/experiments/drift-v2-${stamp}/console.log"

cd "$project_dir"
"$uv" run --no-sync fyp-hardware --require-gpu

run_profile() {
  local suffix="$1"
  shift
  local run_dir="${project_dir}/runs/siamese/${stamp}-realistic-${suffix}"
  local -a resume_args=()
  if [[ -f "${run_dir}/config.json" && -f "${run_dir}/models/resume.pt" ]]; then
    echo "Resuming ${suffix} from ${run_dir}/models/resume.pt"
    resume_args=(--resume-run-dir "$run_dir")
  fi
  "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-train-router \
    --base-checkpoint "$base_checkpoint" \
    --run-id "${stamp}-realistic-${suffix}" \
    "${resume_args[@]}" \
    --long-horizon \
    --adaptation-profile realistic \
    --epochs "$epochs" \
    --batch-size "$batch_size" \
    --seed "$seed" \
    --log-every 50 \
    --evaluate-drift \
    "$@"
}

{
  echo "Drift v2 matched run: ${stamp}"
  echo "Historical checkpoint: ${base_checkpoint}"
  echo "Epochs: ${epochs}; batch size: ${batch_size}; seed: ${seed}"
  echo "Starting saliency-supervised profile"
  run_profile saliency
  echo "Starting matched no-auxiliary-saliency control"
  run_profile noaux --disable-saliency-loss
  echo "Completed drift v2 matched run"
} 2>&1 | tee -a "$log"

echo "Log: $log"

#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Shared-checkpoint patch-only test of learned weighting and auxiliary losses.
set -euo pipefail

project_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
cd "$project_dir"
export PYTHONPATH="${project_dir}/src"
export HF_HOME="${project_dir}/cache/huggingface"

python="${project_dir}/.venv/bin/python"
root="${project_dir}/runs/patch-only-20261031"
logs="${root}/logs"
previous="${project_dir}/runs/validation-20261031/logs/patch-mechanism-progress.log"
mkdir -p "$logs" "$HF_HOME"

record_exit() {
  status="$?"
  printf 'factorial_exit_code=%s ended_utc=%s\n' "$status" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" \
    >> "${logs}/progress.log"
}
trap record_exit EXIT

for attempt in $(seq 1 720); do
  if test -f "$previous" && grep -q '^mechanism_completed=updated ' "$previous"; then
    break
  fi
  if test "$attempt" -eq 720; then
    echo 'Timed out waiting for the legacy-model mechanism audit' >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  sleep 30
done

run_stage() {
  name="$1"
  shift
  printf 'starting=%s at=%s\n' "$name" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" | tee -a "${logs}/progress.log"
  "$@" > "${logs}/${name}.log" 2>&1
  printf 'completed=%s at=%s\n' "$name" "$(date -u +%FT%TZ)" | tee -a "${logs}/progress.log"
}

run_stage historical-patch-only-uniform-seed42 \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.training.classifier \
    --method baseline --pooling-mode patch_only --cv-val-year 2009 \
    --epochs 3 --batch-size 8 --seed 42 --data-root data \
    --output "$root" --run-id historical-patch-only-uniform-seed42 \
    --log-every 100

base="${root}/baseline/historical-patch-only-uniform-seed42/models/best.pt"
test -f "$base"

run_stage updated-patch-only-uniform-seed42 \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.training.fine_tune \
    --base-checkpoint "$base" --epochs 5 --batch-size 8 \
    --selection-fraction 0.5 --seed 42 --data-root data \
    --output "$root" --run-id updated-patch-only-uniform-seed42 \
    --log-every 50 --drift-scenarios \
    clean illumination white_balance sensor_noise defocus_blur motion_blur \
    jpeg_compression resolution_loss viewpoint_shift occlusion realistic_mix

common=(
  --base-checkpoint "$base" --long-horizon
  --adaptation-profile realistic --initialize-saliency-from-uniform
  --epochs 5 --batch-size 8 --selection-fraction 0.5
  --seed 42 --data-root data --output "$root" --log-every 50
  --evaluate-drift --drift-scenarios
  clean illumination white_balance sensor_noise defocus_blur motion_blur
  jpeg_compression resolution_loss viewpoint_shift occlusion realistic_mix
)

run_stage updated-patch-only-saliency-noaux-seed42 \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.training.temporal_router \
    "${common[@]}" --disable-saliency-loss \
    --run-id updated-patch-only-saliency-noaux-seed42

run_stage updated-patch-only-saliency-aux-seed42 \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.training.temporal_router \
    "${common[@]}" \
    --run-id updated-patch-only-saliency-aux-seed42

saliency_run="${root}/siamese/updated-patch-only-saliency-aux-seed42"
run_stage patch-only-router-feature-ablation \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.evaluation.router_feature_ablation \
    --run-dir "$saliency_run" --data-root data \
    --output "${root}/feature-ablation"

run_stage patch-only-updated-mechanism \
  "$python" -m fyp_drift.evaluation.patch_mechanism \
    --run-dir "$saliency_run" --data-root data --year 2014 \
    --batch-size 16 --role updated \
    --output "${root}/patch-mechanism-updated-2014.json"

================================================
FILE: deploy/server.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

project_dir="$(cd -- "$(dirname -- "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/.." && pwd)"
storage_dir="$(dirname -- "$(readlink -f -- "${project_dir}/runs")")"
cache_dir="$(readlink -f -- "${project_dir}/cache")"
action="${1:-status}"
if [[ "$#" -gt 0 ]]; then shift; fi

usage() {
  echo "Usage: bash deploy/server.sh {setup|status|smoke|ingest|audit-data|experiment|baseline|xai|siamese|evaluate|drift|all} [model options]"
  echo "Set FYP_ALLOW_CPU_TRAIN=1 to permit full training without a GPU."
}

if [[ "$action" == "setup" ]]; then
  exec bash "${project_dir}/deploy/remote_setup.sh"
fi
if [[ ! -x "${project_dir}/tools/uv" || ! -d "${storage_dir}/venv" ]]; then
  echo "Run 'bash deploy/server.sh setup' first" >&2
  exit 1
fi

cd "$project_dir"
export UV_CACHE_DIR="${cache_dir}/uv"
export UV_PROJECT_ENVIRONMENT="${storage_dir}/venv"
export HF_HOME="${cache_dir}/huggingface"
mkdir -p "$HF_HOME"
uv="${project_dir}/tools/uv"

latest_best() {
  local method="$1"
  local checkpoint
  checkpoint="$(find "${project_dir}/runs/${method}" -mindepth 3 -maxdepth 3 -path '*/models/best.pt' -type f 2>/dev/null | sort | tail -n 1)"
  if [[ -z "$checkpoint" ]]; then
    echo "No best checkpoint found for ${method}" >&2
    exit 1
  fi
  printf '%s\n' "$checkpoint"
}

check_training_device() {
  if [[ "${FYP_ALLOW_CPU_TRAIN:-0}" != "1" ]]; then
    if ! "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-hardware --require-gpu; then
      echo "Full training requires an accessible GPU by default. Set FYP_ALLOW_CPU_TRAIN=1 to run on CPU." >&2
      exit 2
    fi
  fi
}

train_method() {
  local method="$1"
  shift
  "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-train --method "$method" "$@"
}

train_twin() {
  local base
  base="$(latest_best xai)"
  "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-train-router --base-checkpoint "$base" "$@"
}

case "$action" in
  status)
    git status --short --branch
    "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-hardware
    for name in data runs tools .venv cache; do
      printf '%s -> %s\n' "$name" "$(readlink -f "$name")"
    done
    ;;
  smoke) "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-smoke "$@" ;;
  ingest) "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-ingest "$@" ;;
  audit-data) "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-audit-data "$@" ;;
  experiment) check_training_device; "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-experiment "$@" ;;
  baseline) check_training_device; train_method baseline "$@" ;;
  xai) check_training_device; train_method xai "$@" ;;
  siamese) check_training_device; train_twin "$@" ;;
  evaluate)
    for method in baseline xai; do
      checkpoint="$(latest_best "$method")"
      "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-evaluate --method "$method" --checkpoint "$checkpoint" --output "$(dirname "$(dirname "$checkpoint")")/metrics/recheck" "$@"
    done
    ;;
  drift)
    for method in baseline xai; do
      checkpoint="$(latest_best "$method")"
      run_root="$(dirname "$(dirname "$checkpoint")")"
      "$uv" run --no-sync fyp-evaluate-drift --method "$method" --checkpoint "$checkpoint" \
        --output "${run_root}/metrics/drift" "$@"
    done
    ;;
  all)
    check_training_device
    train_method baseline "$@"
    train_method xai "$@"
    train_twin "$@"
    ;;
  *) usage >&2; exit 2 ;;
esac



================================================
FILE: docs/acdc_adaptation_protocol.md
================================================
# ACDC paired-condition adaptation protocol

## Role of ACDC

ACDC is a realistic adverse-condition driving benchmark with fog, night, rain
and snow domains. Each adverse image is supplied with a normal-condition view
of the same scene, a 19-class semantic segmentation annotation and an invalid
region mask. This makes ACDC useful for testing whether an update learns
condition-invariant evidence rather than merely exploiting a synthetic
corruption.

ACDC is not a chronological stream. It therefore complements CLEAR rather than
replacing its temporal protocol. CLEAR tests deployment over time. ACDC tests
cross-condition adaptation on a safety-relevant dense prediction task.

The dataset requires registration and is licensed for non-commercial use.
Download `rgb_anon_trainvaltest.zip` and `gt_trainval.zip` from the
[official ACDC website](https://acdc.vision.ee.ethz.ch/download), then extract
them beneath `data/ACDC`. Build and validate the paired manifest with:

```bash
tools/uv run fyp-ingest-acdc --data-root data/ACDC
```

The scanner follows the layout used by the official CMA implementation. It
matches each adverse image to its `_rgb_ref_anon.png` normal reference,
`_gt_labelTrainIds.png` semantic label and `_gt_invIds.png` uncertain-region
mask. It fails if a required pair is incomplete.

## What counts as evidence of adaptation

Weight change alone is insufficient. A large parameter update can damage the
model, while a small targeted update can be useful. The evaluation therefore
combines four types of evidence.

1. **Task utility.** Report adverse-condition mIoU and macro F1 for fog, night,
   rain and snow. Report normal-condition scores and the clean-retention change.
2. **Condition invariance.** Compare normal and adverse representations with
   RBF MMD and a cross-validated domain-classifier ROC AUC after row-wise L2
   normalisation. A useful update should reduce both measures.
3. **Matched-scene consistency.** For the same-scene pairs, report cosine
   feature distance and prediction Jensen-Shannon divergence before and after
   adaptation. Use a paired bootstrap confidence interval for the reduction.
4. **Non-collapse and update tracing.** Report feature variance, effective rank,
   relative parameter change and the layers with the largest relative update.
   Lower MMD is not beneficial if the representation has collapsed or task
   performance has fallen.

The command below accepts historical and adapted feature archives. Each NPZ
file must contain paired `normal_features` and `adverse_features` matrices. It
may also contain `normal_probabilities`, `adverse_probabilities`,
`normal_saliency` and `adverse_saliency`.

```bash
tools/uv run fyp-audit-adaptation \
  --historical-features runs/acdc/historical_features.npz \
  --adapted-features runs/acdc/adapted_features.npz \
  --historical-checkpoint runs/acdc/historical.pt \
  --adapted-checkpoint runs/acdc/adapted.pt \
  --output runs/acdc/adaptation_evidence.json
```

The project can extract those archives directly from the historical and adapted
Saliency-guided ViT checkpoints:

```bash
tools/uv run fyp-screen-acdc \
  --manifest data/ACDC/paired_manifest.csv \
  --historical-checkpoint runs/xai/<historical-run>/models/best.pt \
  --adapted-checkpoint runs/siamese/<adapted-run>/models/best.pt \
  --output runs/acdc/paired-screen
```

The default compares historical and adapted saliency-guided checkpoints. To use the
original uniform-pooling ViT as the baseline, add `--historical-method baseline`
and provide its checkpoint. The output records both method names so the
comparison cannot be mistaken for a same-architecture weight update.

This command is deliberately labelled a representation screen. The checkpoints
retain the CLEAR-100 classification head, whose labels have no valid mapping to
ACDC's semantic classes. ACDC task-performance claims require a 19-class ViT
segmentation head and a matched normal-to-adverse source training protocol.

Spatial saliency comparison is disabled by default. The normal and adverse
frames are GPS-matched but are not pixel-aligned. Patch maps should only be
compared after correspondence warping and invalid-region masking, as in
[Contrastive Model Adaptation](https://github.com/brdav/cma). Enable the check
with `--saliency-aligned` only after that preprocessing has been applied.

## Decision rule

The generated JSON separates three findings. `baseline_drift` first records
whether the historical representation rejects the no-drift hypothesis under
the MMD permutation test. `representation_mitigation` then requires lower MMD,
lower domain separability and a paired feature-distance reduction whose 95%
bootstrap interval is above zero. `residual_drift` repeats the MMD test on the
adapted representation. The model can therefore mitigate drift even when some
statistically detectable shift remains.

Feature variance and effective rank ratios guard against a trivial constant
representation. Both default to a retention floor of 0.8. When compatible
labels are present in both NPZ archives, `task_mitigation` additionally requires
an adverse macro F1 gain while allowing at most a 0.01 reduction in normal macro
F1. These thresholds are written into the report and can be changed from the
command line.

An adaptation claim is supported when baseline drift is verified, the
representation criteria pass and the task criterion passes. On ACDC, the
current CLEAR checkpoints can establish representation mitigation only. ACDC
task mitigation remains unassessed until the segmentation extension is run.

This protocol follows the paired normal/adverse rationale of the
[ACDC benchmark](https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/ICCV2021/html/Sakaridis_ACDC_The_Adverse_Conditions_Dataset_With_Correspondences_for_Semantic_Driving_ICCV_2021_paper.html)
and the condition-invariant feature objective of CMA. MMD and the domain
classifier remain external diagnostics: they are not training losses in the
current model and therefore provide a separate check on the claimed behaviour.

FILE: docs/architecture.md
================================================
# Code structure

The project is an installable Python package. Run every Python stage through
`uv run` so local and server executions use the locked environment.

## Package map

```text
src/fyp_drift/
├── data/             CLEAR-100 ingestion, manifests, temporal splits, drift transforms
├── models/           DINOv2 classifier, Siamese comparison, transparent routing policy
├── training/         Historical and saliency-guided training and temporal routing
├── evaluation/       Metrics, frozen evaluations, saliency analysis, counterexample audits
├── artifacts/        Checkpoints, run folders, manual snapshots, report exports
├── visualization/    Drift previews and saliency or router panels
├── workflows/        Multi-stage experiment orchestration
└── system/           Hardware inspection and the synthetic runtime check
```

The folders describe responsibility rather than experiment order. Training code
loads data through `data/`, builds networks from `models/`, writes through
`artifacts/`, and calls metric functions from `evaluation/`. Dataset downloads do
not occur inside a training command.

## Main commands

| Stage | Command | Main output |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Environment | `uv sync --locked` | `.venv/` |
| Data ingestion | `uv run fyp-ingest` | `data/clear100/manifest.csv` |
| Baseline or saliency-guided training | `uv run fyp-train --method baseline` | `runs/baseline/<run-id>/` |
| Conventional temporal fine-tuning | `uv run fyp-train-finetune --base-checkpoint <path>` | `runs/finetune/<run-id>/` |
| Temporal adaptation and routing | `uv run fyp-train-router --base-checkpoint <path>` | `runs/siamese/<run-id>/` |
| Frozen classifier evaluation | `uv run fyp-evaluate ...` | JSON metrics and prediction CSV |
| Controlled drift evaluation | `uv run fyp-evaluate-drift ...` | Per-source temporal metrics |
| Class-level router analysis | `uv run fyp-export-classwise ...` | Per-class and historical-difficulty CSV tables |
| Counterexample audit | `uv run fyp-audit-router ...` | Decision CSV and bounded panels |
| Runtime check | `uv run fyp-smoke` | Temporary synthetic run |
| Hardware check | `uv run fyp-hardware` | Torch, CUDA, GPU, CPU, and storage status |

Run `uv run <command> --help` for the full arguments. `pyproject.toml` is the
single command registry used on Windows and Ubuntu.

## Experiment boundary

The chronological pipeline has five separate decisions:

1. Fit the historical classifier on earlier years.
2. Select its checkpoint on a later validation year.
3. Adapt either a uniform-pooling ViT with classification loss only or a copied
   saliency-guided ViT on the declared adaptation window.
4. Select the updated checkpoint and fit the router on disjoint calibration data.
5. Freeze both models and the router before evaluating future years.

`training/classifier.py` owns steps 1 and 2. `training/fine_tune.py` provides the
conventional adaptation control. `training/temporal_router.py` owns the
saliency-guided adaptation and routing path. Metrics and figures consume saved
artifacts; they do not retrain a model.

## Stored artifacts

Each run has the same small set of directories:

```text
<run-id>/
├── config.json
├── logs/
├── metrics/
├── models/
└── manual/
```

`models/best.pt` records the validation-selected weights. `models/last.pt`
records the final epoch. Temporal router runs also update `models/resume.pt`
atomically after every completed epoch. Routing CSV files retain the four policy
features, the fitted logistic routing score, the chosen tower, and both model predictions.
The score has not been independently checked for probability calibration.

## Deployment scripts

`bootstrap.ps1` prepares and runs the project on Windows. `deploy/server.sh`
provides the matching Ubuntu commands. `deploy/launch.sh` adds logged `tmux`
execution for long jobs. The scripts call package entry points and contain no
duplicate model logic.

# Code map

`docs/code_digest.txt` is a full plain-text digest of the code (generated with
[gitingest](https://github.com/coderamp-labs/gitingest): `uvx gitingest . -o docs/code_digest.txt -e "results/*" -e "figures/*" -e "models/*"`),
handy for searching the code or giving it to an LLM. The diagram below is the architecture view in the style of
[GitDiagram](https://gitdiagram.com); GitHub renders it directly.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
    subgraph data["Data (fyp_drift.data, fmow_v2.expand_subset)"]
        S3[("fMoW-RGB<br/>public S3")] --> SUB["Country-matched subset<br/>14k images / period"]
        SUB --> META["Acquisition metadata<br/>sun, cloud cover"]
        SUB --> CACHE["280 px crop cache<br/>(uint8 memmap)"]
    end
    subgraph study["Base study (evaluation/fmow_dinov2l_study.py)"]
        CACHE --> BB["Frozen DINOv2-L/14<br/>+ optional LoRA"]
        BB --> HIST["Historical head<br/>(2010-13)"]
        BB --> DRIFT["Drift + harm gate<br/>MMD, domain AUC"]
        CACHE --> CLOUD["Cloud overlay +<br/>held-out cumulus"]
    end
    subgraph heads["Heads on cached tokens"]
        BB --> TOK["Token caches<br/>10x10 cells"]
        TOK --> ATT["Learned attention<br/>saliency_attention_study"]
        TOK --> SAL["Hard / soft saliency gates<br/>soft_saliency_heads"]
        HIST -->|"evidence z"| SAL
    end
    subgraph v2["fmow_v2 additions"]
        BB --> LORA["Equal-training LoRA<br/>equal_lora.py"]
        BB --> HYB["LoRA + soft gate<br/>hybrid.py"]
        ATT & SAL --> TESTS["Moved object, clouds,<br/>saliency in training"]
        ATT & SAL & HYB --> EXPL["Exact explanations<br/>explain.py"]
        ATT & SAL --> ROUTE["Auditable router<br/>saliency_router_study"]
    end
    subgraph out["Outputs"]
        LORA & HYB & TESTS & EXPL & ROUTE --> RES[("results/fmow_v2")]
        RES --> FIG["figures/fmow_v2<br/>(plots/ draws every figure)"]
        ATT & SAL & LORA & HYB --> MOD[("models/fmow_v2<br/>safetensors")]
        MOD --> PRED["models.py predict<br/>inference + attention map"]
    end
```

## Packages

| Path | What it does |
|---|---|
| `src/fyp_drift/data/` | Dataset loaders (CLEAR, corruptions, temporal sampling) and fMoW metadata download |
| `src/fyp_drift/models/` | ViT classifier, Siamese router and routing models from the earlier CLEAR-100 study |
| `src/fyp_drift/training/` | Training loops for the earlier study (classifier, fine-tuning, router, saliency pilots) |
| `src/fyp_drift/evaluation/` | Experiments. fMoW: `fmow_dinov2l_study.py` (data, backbone, clouds, LoRA, drift), `saliency_attention_study.py` and `soft_saliency_heads.py` (heads), `saliency_router_study.py` (router), `extra_metrics.py`, `fmow_token_dropping.py`, `strict_saliency_pooling.py` |
| `src/fyp_drift/evaluation/fmow_v2/` | v2 additions: equal-training LoRA, hybrid, held-out clouds, explanations, model export and inference |
| `src/fyp_drift/artifacts/`, `system/` | Run storage, reports, hardware checks |
| `scripts/fmow_v2/` | Environment file and the equal-training runner |
| `scripts/pilots/` | One-off pilot scripts from 3 Nov, kept for the record (superseded by `evaluation/`) |
| `deploy/` | Server setup and batch scripts for the earlier study |
| `tests/` | Unit tests (`pytest`) |
| `results/fmow_v2/` | Saved output of every v2 run, with per-image predictions |
| `figures/fmow_v2/` | Every figure plus the code and data that draw it |
| `models/fmow_v2/` | Trained models (safetensors) for inference |

## v2 modules (`src/fyp_drift/evaluation/fmow_v2/`)

| Module | Role |
|---|---|
| `expand_subset.py` | Download the 14k-per-period country-matched subset |
| `tokens.py` | Build the frozen-token caches (`cloud`, `pool`, `heldout` steps) |
| `context.py` | Shared splits, image cache, cloud settings and frozen head |
| `equal_lora.py` | LoRA with the heads' selection protocol (`--rank --blocks --condition --seed`) |
| `hybrid.py` | LoRA + soft saliency gate trained end to end |
| `shift_test.py` | Moved-object grounding test |
| `cloud_pooling.py` | Cloud-aware pooling and the head-width sweep |
| `saliency_learning.py` | Saliency in the training signal only |
| `analysis.py` | Map stability, real-cloud scores, example maps |
| `explain.py` | Exact per-cell and per-patch explanations (`maps`, `patches` steps) |
| `models.py` | Save, load, export (`export`) and run (`predict`) trained models |

FILE: docs/data.md

# Data

**Source:** fMoW-RGB, public on AWS at `s3://spacenet-dataset/Hosted-Datasets/fmow/fmow-rgb/` (HTTPS mirror
`https://spacenet-dataset.s3.amazonaws.com/Hosted-Datasets/fmow/fmow-rgb/`). Each image comes with a JSON file
holding the object box, timestamp, sun angles, off-nadir angle and cloud cover.

**Subset.** `fmow_v2.expand_subset` picks about 14,000 images for each period with the same per-country quota in
every period, so geography does not change between periods. 31 of about 42,000 downloads failed.

| Period | Role | Split (by location) | Images |
|---|---|---|---|
| 2010–13 | Train the historical model | hist_train / hist_val | 11,790 / 2,193 |
| 2014–15 | Adapt (update) the model | adapt_train / adapt_val | 12,572 / 1,420 |
| 2016–17 | Test | future_test (half of the locations) | 7,077 |

Splits are by location group (country + UTM zone), so no site appears in two splits.

**Preparation.** Each image is cropped to a square twice the object box's longer side (at least a quarter, at
most all of the shorter side) and resized to 280 × 280. Crops are cached once as a uint8 memmap
(`$FYP_CACHE`), and frozen DINOv2-L tokens are cached per split (`$FYP_TOKENS`, pooled to 10 × 10 cells).

**Clouds.** Training overlay: near-white cloud on the brightest 20% of a smoothed noise field (α = 0.85).
Held-out test clouds: cumulus with sun-consistent shadows, beside or over the object. Real clouds: test images
whose metadata cloud cover is at least 10%.



================================================
FILE: docs/drift_validation.md
================================================
# Drift validation and external replication

## What is being verified

The verification step asks whether a reference image sample and a later or
corrupted sample produce different frozen DINOv2 feature distributions. It uses
two tests whose outputs can be checked independently:

1. **Maximum mean discrepancy (MMD).** An RBF-kernel MMD squared value records
   the observed separation. A permutation test estimates the probability of a
   separation at least this large if the samples were exchangeable.
2. **Classifier two-sample test.** A balanced logistic classifier is trained and
   evaluated by five-fold cross-validation to distinguish reference from target
   features. The saved AUC is the raw out-of-fold ROC AUC for the assigned
   domain labels; it is not symmetrised. A value of 0.5 is chance ranking,
   values above 0.5 rank the target domain higher, and values below 0.5 rank
   it lower. Interpret departures in either direction with sampling uncertainty.

The code is intentionally limited to these two measures. Gretton et al. define
the kernel two-sample test, while Lopez-Paz and Oquab describe the classifier
two-sample test. Alibi Detect provides public `MMDDrift` and `ClassifierDrift`
implementations against which the methodology can be inspected.

- Gretton et al.: <https://www.jmlr.org/papers/v13/gretton12a.html>
- Lopez-Paz and Oquab: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1610.06545>
- Alibi Detect source: <https://github.com/SeldonIO/alibi-detect>
- Alibi Detect drift documentation: <https://docs.seldon.ai/alibi-detect/cd>

## CLEAR controls

Three comparisons prevent a significant result from being treated as sufficient
evidence on its own:

- a seeded random clean split-half control estimates within-distribution
  variation without retaining dataset order;
- clean historical and future years measure natural temporal separation;
- clean and shifted versions of the same 2014 images isolate each controlled
  corruption from image identity and class composition.

The paired design is part of sampling, even though MMD and the domain classifier
remain distribution-level tests. A corruption is measurable when its MMD is
larger than the null control, its permutation p-value is small, and its domain
ROC AUC rises above chance. It is harmful only when classification performance
also falls. This distinction avoids presenting any visible change as harmful
data drift.

### Unlabelled sample-size sensitivity

The earlier 300-image audit balanced samples by class retrospectively. Its MMD
estimate used all 300 images per group, but each permutation test subsampled
200 per group. The new `fyp_drift.evaluation.drift_sample_size` audit selects
images randomly within year without class labels and uses the full sampled
group for each of 499 permutations. It tests nested samples from one seed, so
the rows are related rather than independent replications.

| Images per group | Clean 2009 vs 2014 MMD p | Clean 2014 null p | Motion blur p | Evidence-ranked mix p |
| ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| 100 | 0.268 | 0.898 | 0.002 | 0.002 |
| 300 | 0.058 | 0.514 | 0.002 | 0.002 |
| 500 | 0.136 | 0.678 | 0.002 | 0.002 |

Motion-blur AUC is 0.989–0.994 and mixture AUC is 0.999–1.000; the clean-year
AUC range is 0.457–0.565. The controlled changes are detectable here even with
100 images per group. The clean-year non-detection through 500 images is a
limit of this representation, draw, and detector setup, not evidence that
natural drift is absent. Random sampling may also vary the class mixture.
The complete record is in
`results/validation-20261031/drift-sample-size-100-300-500.json`.

## WILDS replication

`fyp-replicate-wilds` repeats the detector and performance analysis on standard
WILDS splits. It fits a linear classifier to frozen DINOv2 training features and
reports accuracy and macro F1 on in-distribution and out-of-distribution
validation data.

The optional `--architecture-screen` adds a matched comparison between the
uniform-patch baseline and the learned saliency gate. It trains the task heads
over the same frozen backbone, images, seed, epochs, and optimiser. This provides
a direct, affordable test of the modified classifier across datasets while
avoiding the false claim that a CLEAR-calibrated router transfers unchanged.

FMoW is the first choice because it is an image classification dataset with
time and geography in its distribution shift. iWildCam is a useful secondary
test of camera-location shift. Camelyon17 tests hospital acquisition shift, but
it is not temporal. The WILDS repository supplies the dataset loaders and split
definitions: <https://github.com/p-lambda/wilds>.

The resulting JSON separates two claims:

- MMD and domain ROC AUC show whether the frozen feature distribution changed;
- the ID-to-OOD accuracy and macro-F1 differences show whether that change
  reduced predictive performance.

This screen does not by itself reproduce the saliency-guided router. A full
architecture replication must retrain and calibrate the historical model,
updated model, and router on each dataset with matched seeds and budgets.

Wild-Time Yearbook is included as a small, downloadable temporal check when the
larger WILDS archives are unavailable. It trains on 1930-1989, evaluates an ID
window from 1980-1989, and evaluates a later window from 2004-2013. Its binary,
32-pixel portrait task is useful for checking temporal reproducibility but does
not offer the visual complexity or operational setting of FMoW.

## CIFAR-10.1 replication

CIFAR-10.1 is a separately collected replacement test set for CIFAR-10. It
provides a small natural-shift check when large WILDS archives are unavailable.
It is not a temporal dataset, so its results are described as cross-collection
reproduction shift.

```bash
uv run fyp-replicate-cifar \
  --data-root data/cifar \
  --output runs/cifar/cifar10-1-screen \
  --train-limit 5000 \
  --eval-limit 1000 \
  --epochs 2
```

The command compares the original CIFAR-10 test set with CIFAR-10.1 v6 using
the same frozen-feature probe, MMD test, domain classifier, and matched baseline
and saliency-gated ViT heads used by the other replication scripts.

The three-seed screen found MMD permutation p = 0.005 for every seed and domain
ROC AUC between 0.589 and 0.606. Mean OOD macro F1 was 73.97% for the uniform
baseline and 74.27% for the saliency head. The mean difference was +0.30 points
with a sample standard deviation of 0.11 points; all three seed differences were
positive. This is a small, repeatable benefit on a natural recollection shift,
not evidence of a large or universal improvement.

## Camelyon17 replication

Camelyon17-WILDS tests hospital acquisition shift in histopathology patches. A
public Hugging Face mirror is used because the official CodaLab certificate has
expired. The mirror groups the source hospitals in one training split, so the
screen creates a class-balanced, patient-disjoint source holdout for ID
evaluation and uses the validation hospital as the OOD sample.

```bash
uv run fyp-replicate-camelyon \
  --output runs/wilds/camelyon17/seed42 \
  --train-limit 5000 \
  --eval-limit 1000 \
  --epochs 2 \
  --seed 42
```

The experiment is a non-standard WILDS screen. It uses an externally pretrained
DINOv2 backbone and three seeds rather than the ten Camelyon17 replicates
requested for a standard leaderboard submission. Both compared heads share
those deviations, so the within-run architecture contrast remains controlled.

Across seeds 42-44, MMD squared ranged from 0.0237 to 0.0566 with permutation
p = 0.005 in every run. Domain ROC AUC ranged from 0.941 to 0.995. Mean OOD
macro F1 was 77.31% for the uniform baseline and 77.24% for the saliency head.
The mean difference was -0.07 points with a sample standard deviation of 0.12.
One run tied and two were slightly negative. Camelyon17 verifies a large
acquisition shift but does not verify an accuracy benefit from the current
saliency head.



================================================
FILE: docs/examiner_review.md
================================================
# Examiner review of the draft paper

## Overall assessment

The project has a credible undergraduate contribution: it links a trainable
patch weighting mechanism to prediction, compares historical and updated ViTs,
and records the evidence used by a simple routing policy. The implementation is
more substantial than a post-hoc saliency demonstration. The strongest result
is the source-separated CLEAR analysis, especially the matched control without
the auxiliary saliency losses.

The draft does not yet support a general claim that XAI improves drift
correction. Its current evidence supports a narrower conclusion: the saliency
mechanism improves selected severe CLEAR conditions, has a small positive effect
on CIFAR-10.1, does not improve Yearbook, and is neutral to slightly negative on
Camelyon17-WILDS. The paper will become stronger if it makes that claim hierarchy
explicit and treats the external screens as tests of the classifier component
rather than replications of the full router.

## Major points

### 1. Separate the three claims

The draft sometimes moves between three propositions:

1. the saliency-gated classifier can outperform uniform patch pooling;
2. temporal adaptation can outperform the historical model;
3. the transparent router can select beneficial updates.

The experiments do not test all three propositions on every dataset. CLEAR
tests the complete chain. Yearbook, CIFAR-10.1 and Camelyon17 test only matched
frozen-backbone heads and feature drift. Each result table should therefore name
the component tested and the inference it permits.

### 2. Treat the reported CLEAR run as exploratory

The published CLEAR tables reuse 2011 labels for adapted-checkpoint selection
and router calibration. The corrected code uses disjoint class-stratified
partitions, but the main numerical result has not been rerun under that protocol.
This is the largest threat to the paper's internal validity. The text should not
describe the archived thresholds as confirmatory. A final submission should
rerun at least three seeds with the corrected split and report paired confidence
intervals over image-level score differences.

### 3. Avoid post-hoc success thresholds

The two-point criterion was added after the results were available, while the
three-point target was not registered outside the project. Both values can be
useful descriptive reference points, but neither functions as a statistical
decision rule. The paper should label them as engineering targets and give
uncertainty intervals and repeated-seed effects greater evidential weight.

### 4. Prove that saliency adds information beyond confidence

The current loss ablation asks whether saliency supervision helps a
saliency-gated model. It does not answer whether saliency features improve the
router beyond confidence and entropy. The code now exposes confidence-only,
saliency-only and combined policies, but the paper has no matched result table
for them. RQ3 must remain unresolved until those policies are compared at equal
route coverage or equal harmful-route rate.

### 5. Qualify the word Siamese

The model is an asymmetric paired comparison between two versioned ViTs. It is
not a conventional Siamese metric learner trained with a contrastive or triplet
loss. Retaining the term is reasonable if the methods section states this
difference whenever the architecture is introduced. The paired design is
justified by the need to compute prediction and evidence changes against a
recoverable historical model. It should not be presented as inherently more
accurate than a single updated ViT.

### 6. Tighten drift verification

MMD, a classifier two-sample test and task performance answer different
questions. The paper handles this distinction well, but its joint detection
rule is project-specific. Report the clean null, effect size and p-value without
turning the rule into a universal definition of drift. For patch datasets,
sampling and cross-validation should respect patient or slide groups; otherwise
nearby patches can leak acquisition information across folds. The Camelyon17
screen uses patient-disjoint source partitions for this reason.

### 7. Place the external results in the results section

The detailed Yearbook and CIFAR numbers currently appear inside the protocol
section. Move them to a cross-dataset results subsection. Keep the methods
section for datasets, splits, metrics and restrictions. A compact protocol table,
a drift table and an architecture table will make the evidence easier to audit.

### 8. State the benchmark deviations

The WILDS submission rules require ten Camelyon17 replicates and prohibit
external pretraining for a standard submission. This project uses DINOv2 and
three seeds, so its Camelyon17 result is a non-standard exploratory screen. The
paper should state this before presenting the numbers. The comparison remains
useful because both heads share the same backbone, data and optimisation budget.
It should not be compared directly with the WILDS leaderboard.

### 9. Strengthen citation logic

Several references establish that a technique exists but do not establish that
it will work in this setting. The paper should distinguish three uses of a
citation:

- a benchmark paper supports the dataset and split definition;
- a methods paper supports a design choice or metric;
- the present experiments determine whether that choice helps here.

The literature can motivate saliency guidance, paired transformers and drift
tests. It cannot substitute for the missing router-feature ablation or repeated
CLEAR seeds. Claims about Camelyon17 should cite the WILDS benchmark and the
original CAMELYON17 challenge rather than a dataset mirror alone.

## Recommended submission position

The paper should present the router as an auditable model-version selection
prototype with conditional accuracy benefits. That conclusion follows from the
available evidence and leaves room for negative results. A stronger claim that
XAI provides general drift correction should wait for the corrected CLEAR runs,
the router-feature ablation and a complete external router replication.

## Sources used for this review

- WILDS benchmark and dataset definitions: <https://proceedings.mlr.press/v139/koh21a.html>
- WILDS submission rules and replicate requirements: <https://wilds.stanford.edu/submit/>
- CAMELYON17 challenge: <https://pubmed.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/30716025/>
- Kernel two-sample testing: <https://www.jmlr.org/papers/v13/gretton12a.html>
- Classifier two-sample testing: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1610.06545>
- Interpretable-model argument: <https://doi.org/10.1038/s42256-019-0048-x>



================================================
FILE: docs/experiment_protocol.md
================================================
# Experiment protocol: source-separated acquisition and context shifts

## Three separate experiments

1. **Unlabelled drift detection.** Given a limited sample of incoming raw
   images, embed them with the classifier's frozen, externally pretrained
   DINOv2 backbone and ask
   whether their distribution differs from a historical sample. MMD with a
   permutation test is the primary detector; grouped domain-classifier AUC is
   a complementary detector motivated by classifier two-sample tests. Report
   detection and effect-size estimates at fixed sample counts, including a
   clean split-half null. The observed failure to detect clean natural-year
   change with 300 samples is a power/representation boundary, not proof that
   no drift exists. Neither detector uses class labels or establishes task harm.
2. **Saliency-guided updating.** On the fixed chronology, compare the
   conventional uniform-pooling model, learned patch weighting without the
   auxiliary objectives, and learned weighting with those objectives. Report
   clean and every source-specific future condition. A fixed-checkpoint
   intervention then replaces learned weights with uniform and permuted
   weights while holding the class token, backbone, and classifier fixed. This
   tests whether the trained prediction depends on the map. A separately
   trained `patch_only` pooling variant removes the direct class-token-to-head
   bypass for both uniform and saliency arms. Both updated arms start from the
   same historical patch-only uniform checkpoint; the saliency gate starts at
   exact uniform weights. This removes the different-historical-head objection
   from the new arm comparison. The backbone still retains transformer attention
   between class and patch tokens, so this is a correction of the direct head
   path, not proof that patch features exclude all class-token information.
   Neither intervention alone shows that the map causes a generalisation gain.
3. **Selective routing.** Given the frozen historical and updated saliency
   models from experiment 2, test whether the routing policy adds useful
   decision value beyond always-updated and confidence-only choices. Compare
   policies at matched route coverage, report harmful and missed switches,
   and state the cost or risk target before making a safety claim. Routing is
   a separate decision experiment, not the measure of saliency's effect on
   the updated classifier.

The completed corrected-split conventional arm scores 81.55% macro F1 on the
2014 evidence-ranked mixture, versus 80.78% for updated saliency and 80.81%
for saliency without auxiliary losses. The saliency update is below conventional
fine-tuning on nine of eleven 2014 conditions, tied on one, and above on one.
The archived 1.20-point saliency advantage therefore does not replicate in
this corrected single-seed strategy comparison. The heads begin from different
historical checkpoints; the queued shared-checkpoint `patch_only` factorial
is the required component-level test.

## Why the earlier combined condition was inadequate

The original `combined` transform applied saturation reduction, Gaussian noise,
and a square occlusion to every evaluated image. It was deterministic and useful
as a reproducible stress test, but its panels visually overemphasized missing
pixels. It also mixed distinct causes into one score, making it difficult to say
whether a model handled sensor degradation, illumination, viewpoint, or content
loss. The old transform remains available for exact reproduction and is labelled
legacy in the code.

CLEAR already provides the primary natural temporal shift through images uploaded
from 2004 to 2014. The transforms below are controlled acquisition stressors on
top of that real chronology. They are not estimates of how frequently each event
occurs in a particular industry.

## Source-separated benchmark

| Scenario | Plausible source | Progressive severities |
|---|---|---|
| `clean` | Natural CLEAR temporal evolution only | No synthetic change |
| `illumination` | Exposure, lighting, time of day | ±6/12/18% brightness plus a smaller contrast change |
| `white_balance` | Camera or ISP colour temperature | ±4/8/12% red-blue channel gain |
| `sensor_noise` | Shot and read noise from sensor or low light | Signal-dependent mild noise |
| `defocus_blur` | Autofocus error or depth of field | 3/5/7 pixel Gaussian kernel |
| `motion_blur` | Camera or subject motion | 3/5/7 pixel directional kernel |
| `jpeg_compression` | Re-encoding or bandwidth limits | JPEG quality 85/70/55 |
| `resolution_loss` | Sensor change, downsampling, bandwidth | 192/160/128 pixel intermediate resolution |
| `viewpoint_shift` | Mounting, framing, crop, pose | Small translation, rotation, and scale change |
| `occlusion` | Temporary obstruction or missing content | 1.5/3/5% local mean patch |
| `realistic_mix` | Several changes in the same deployment period | Top 1/2/3 sources from the frozen 2011 evidence ranking |

The 2011 source screen ranks individual transformations by the mean of their
descending MMD-squared and domain-AUC ranks. The top three are motion blur,
sensor noise, and JPEG compression. The final mixed condition applies the top
one, two, or three sources as severity rises and logs those names for every
sample. The 2012-2014 evaluation labels do not influence this selection.

## Adaptation and frozen evaluation

The `realistic` adaptation profile retains the established chronology:

1. Historical model training: 2004–2008.
2. Historical checkpoint selection: 2009.
3. Newer-model adaptation: 2010.
4. Adapted-checkpoint selection and router calibration: disjoint, deterministic,
   class-stratified partitions of 2011 (50/50 by default).
5. Frozen evaluation: 2012–2014.

The 2010 and 2011 streams contain 25% clean examples and 75% examples with one
individual source. Each of the nine individual sources occupies 1/12 of the
stream, so occlusion is 8.3% rather than being present in every image. Compound
mixtures remain held out until final evaluation. The 2012, 2013, and 2014 tests
use severity levels one, two, and three respectively.

This partition prevents the same labels from selecting the adapted checkpoint
and fitting the route coefficients and thresholds. Earlier reported runs predate
this correction and must be identified as exploratory shared-calibration results.
Use `--reuse-calibration-for-selection` only to reproduce those archived runs.

The corrected conventional fine-tuning control uses a historical
uniform-pooling checkpoint trained on 2004–2008 and selected on 2009. It keeps
the saliency arm's 2010 realistic adaptation transform, five-epoch budget,
last-three-block tuning, optimiser rates, seed, and class-stratified 2011
checkpoint-selection partition. Its configuration now records both the
selection and held-out calibration counts and rejects a resume with a different
calibration protocol. `deploy/run_corrected_finetune_20261031.sh` trains this
control and evaluates the same 11 named conditions in 2012–2014. This is a
strategy comparison: the uniform and saliency heads differ in architecture and
historical checkpoint, so it does not isolate a saliency mechanism by itself.

## When saliency guidance is expected to help

The strongest rationale is class-conditional covariate drift. The label meaning
and relevant object evidence remain stable, but background, capture conditions,
or low-level appearance change. A saliency-gated model may then reduce reliance
on a historical contextual shortcut and preserve class evidence from stable
patches. This is especially plausible for framing, localized obstruction, and
background or viewpoint change.

The rationale is weaker for uniform global degradations such as strong blur or
JPEG compression. Saliency cannot recover visual information that has been lost;
any gain there may come from ordinary robustness training rather than the
explanation mechanism. Results must therefore be reported by source, with a
matched no-auxiliary-saliency control, rather than only as one mixed score.

The method is not intended to solve label-definition drift, new classes, severe
object disappearance, or annotation errors. A faithful saliency map can still
explain a prediction made under the wrong task definition.

## Router evidence ablation

The router can be calibrated with prediction evidence, explanation evidence, or
both. This supports a matched test of whether saliency contributes beyond changes
in confidence and predictive entropy:

```bash
tools/uv run fyp-train-router ... --router-features confidence_only
tools/uv run fyp-train-router ... --router-features saliency_only
tools/uv run fyp-train-router ... --router-features combined
```

Prediction-only calibration omits the saliency eligibility floor. Saliency-only
and combined calibration retain it, and every saved policy records the features
used to fit its coefficients.

For a matched feature test after a corrected long-horizon training run has
finished, fit all three policies against the same frozen model pair and 2011
calibration images without retraining:

```bash
tools/uv run python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.router_feature_ablation \
  --run-dir runs/siamese/<corrected-run> \
  --output runs/feature-ablations/<new-id>
```

This command requires the source run's `logs/drift_routing_decisions.csv` and
refuses a legacy shared-calibration run. It compares the native fitted policies
and ranks disagreeing predictions at fixed 10%, 25%, and 50% route coverage.
The latter comparison uses the same number of routes in every feature set and
does not tune thresholds on future labels. It is a transductive diagnostic: it
uses the full unlabelled test group to set a route quota and is not a streaming
deployment policy. The logged future features are rounded to six decimal
places. The command requires those values to reproduce every original policy
decision; if rounding changes one, evaluate the future images again with
full-precision features. The refitted policy must also match the source policy
on the full-precision calibration rows.

For the completed corrected fixed-model comparison, the retrospective
decision-cost summary is in `results/validation-20261031/routing_utility.json`.
It uses beneficial replacements minus lambda times harmful replacements,
relative to always-historical use. On the 2014 evidence-ranked mixture, the
available pair has 379 beneficial and 198 harmful replacements. At lambda=1,
always-updated leads; at lambda=2, confidence-only leads; at lambda=3,
always-historical leads. The combined saliency-and-confidence gate is not the
best option at any of those costs, including at matched 50% disagreement
coverage. Lambda is hypothetical and selected for sensitivity analysis after
seeing the results, so this is not a deployment utility estimate.

## Independent drift verification

Classification loss does not establish that two input distributions differ.
The drift verification command therefore embeds images with the frozen
DINOv2-small backbone and compares distributions before using model accuracy to
judge harm. It reports two complementary quantities:

- RBF maximum mean discrepancy squared, with a permutation p-value;
- cross-validated domain-classifier ROC AUC.

The 2014 source tests pair every shifted image with its clean counterpart. This
controls image identity and class composition when verifying motion blur,
occlusion, and the other acquisition changes. A clean 2014 split-half comparison
provides a within-distribution reference. Natural temporal comparisons use clean
2009 images as the historical reference and clean 2012-2014 images as future
samples. Those comparisons capture temporal change but may also reflect changes
in class frequency and image composition, so they are reported separately.

Run the verification before the model comparison:

```bash
tools/uv run fyp-verify-drift \
  --data-root data \
  --output runs/drift-verification \
  --limit 500 \
  --permutations 499
```

No single value proves harmful drift. MMD tests distribution equality and the
domain classifier measures how reliably held-out features reveal their source.
The saved AUC is the raw out-of-fold value for the assigned domain labels.
Values below 0.5 indicate reverse ranking under that score direction; they
must not be described as symmetrised evidence of no drift.
Performance loss and routing outcomes determine whether an observed shift
matters to the task.

MMD follows the kernel two-sample test of Gretton et al. The domain detector
follows the classifier two-sample test of Lopez-Paz and Oquab. Both methods have
independent open-source implementations in Alibi Detect as `MMDDrift` and
`ClassifierDrift`, which provides a public reference for the test design:

- <https://www.jmlr.org/papers/v13/gretton12a.html>
- <https://arxiv.org/abs/1610.06545>
- <https://github.com/SeldonIO/alibi-detect>

## Cross-dataset replication screen

Run the same feature-space tests and a frozen-DINOv2 linear probe on official
WILDS splits:

```bash
tools/uv run fyp-replicate-wilds \
  --dataset fmow \
  --data-root data/wilds \
  --output runs/wilds/fmow \
  --architecture-screen \
  --download
```

FMoW is the primary external dataset because its satellite imagery includes
explicit temporal and geographical shift. The probe is fitted on `train`,
measured on `id_val`, and then measured on the out-of-distribution `val` split.
MMD and domain-classifier ROC AUC compare the two validation feature samples.
The change in accuracy and macro F1 determines whether the detected shift is
harmful to the probe.

With `--architecture-screen`, the command also trains the baseline and
saliency-gated classifier heads over the same frozen DINOv2 backbone. Both see
the same sampled training images, epochs, seed, and optimiser settings. Their
ID and OOD macro F1 scores test whether the modified classifier changes
generalisation under the dataset's native shift. The screen freezes the
backbone to keep multi-dataset runs practical, so its result must be kept
separate from the partially fine-tuned CLEAR experiment.

iWildCam can test camera-location shift and Camelyon17 can test hospital shift
with the same command. They broaden the evidence but do not verify temporal
replication. A full claim about router transfer requires matched training,
calibration, seeds, and saliency audits on the chosen dataset; this lightweight
screen deliberately makes the narrower claim that the detection and
performance-evaluation protocol can be repeated.

## Evidence base

- Hendrycks and Dietterich group common corruptions into noise, blur, weather,
  and digital categories in ImageNet-C: <https://arxiv.org/abs/1903.12261>.
- The official ImageNet-C implementation uses Gaussian standard deviations
  0.08, 0.12, 0.18, 0.26, and 0.38 on RGB values scaled to [0, 1]; this pilot
  predeclares its first three levels: <https://github.com/hendrycks/robustness/blob/master/ImageNet-C/imagenet_c/imagenet_c/corruptions.py>.
- CLEAR supplies smooth natural temporal evolution and near-future streaming
  evaluation rather than artificial year-by-year IID splits:
  <https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.06289>.
- WILDS documents real shifts across camera locations, geography, institutions,
  and time: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2012.07421>.
- Beery et al. show poor recognition generalization across new camera-trap
  locations where backgrounds and viewpoints change:
  <https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content_ECCV_2018/html/Beery_Recognition_in_Terra_ECCV_2018_paper.html>.
- ImageNet-ES studies environmental and camera-sensor shifts captured with a
  physical camera, illustrating the gap between digital corruption and actual
  acquisition changes:
  <https://openaccess.thecvf.com/content/CVPR2024/html/Baek_Unexplored_Faces_of_Robustness_and_Out-of-Distribution_Covariate_Shifts_in_Environment_CVPR_2024_paper.html>.
- Zheng et al. motivate saliency-guided adaptation when background variation
  dominates domain change: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12712>.
- Wang et al. establish entropy minimization as a fully test-time adaptation
  baseline; our online LoRA entropy arm is a distinct lightweight variant:
  <https://arxiv.org/abs/2006.10726>.

## Pre-run checks

- Confirm every transform is deterministic for a fixed seed and manifest index.
- Save a visual grid with clean and every severity-three source before training.
- Confirm `realistic_mix` uses the frozen 2011 top-three source ranking.
- Train the realistic profile and a matched control without auxiliary saliency
  losses using the same seed, epochs, data, and trainable blocks.
- Report each individual source, the mixed condition, clean performance, helpful
  and harmful routes, and component-stratified results.

## FMoW chronological saliency-correction study

The custom FMoW-RGB subset is a chronological extension, not an official
FMoW-Time benchmark. Its current manifest contains 7,000 images in each of
three windows: historical (2010–2013), adaptation (2014–2015), and future test
(2016–2017). Sampling matches the class-by-country composition across all
three windows. The source files and exact manifest are retained on the GPU
server; the images and report artifacts are not part of the Git branch.

Run the clean historical-to-future audit before any injected corruption or
adaptation. Report frozen-DINOv2 feature MMD² with a permutation test and a
cross-validated domain-classifier ROC AUC, alongside a clean split-half null
comparison. Report accuracy, macro F1, balanced accuracy, and per-class scores
for a historical-only classifier on later clean windows. Include confidence
intervals clustered by geographic tile/sequence where supported, and adjust
the family of drift tests for multiple comparisons. A distribution shift is
not itself evidence of task harm; the later label metrics establish whether
the detected change affects classification. The class-country match controls
two major composition effects but cannot eliminate every geographic or
acquisition confound. A stricter country-by-UTM matched subset is a planned
sensitivity analysis.

After the clean audit, compare these adaptation arms from the same historical
initialization and on the same 2014–2015 labeled adaptation data, seed, and
epoch count:

1. Frozen historical ViT, with no correction.
2. Standard LoRA adaptation.
3. Saliency-guided adaptation without LoRA, using a trainable patch-evidence
   correction while keeping the backbone fixed.
4. LoRA plus saliency-guided adaptation, to test whether saliency adds value
   beyond ordinary LoRA.
5. Label-free test-time entropy adaptation as an additional comparator; it
   must not read future labels and is reported as a transductive/online arm.
   This is TENT-inspired but updates LoRA parameters online rather than
   reproducing TENT's normalization-affine update exactly.

Use the 2016–2017 labels once for the frozen final evaluation. Do not select
epochs, saliency settings, or corruption severities on those labels. Include a
matched random-patch control for any saliency-guided loss so a gain can be
attributed to saliency rather than simply to masking or auxiliary training.
The saliency-only and random-mask-only arms share a head-only parameter budget;
standard LoRA and saliency-LoRA share the same adapters and trainable head.
Report each arm's parameter count, paired per-image and geographic-clustered
uncertainty for score differences, and the saliency faithfulness checks already
used by the project.

Treat synthetic drift as a separate controlled stress test. Cross time with
fixed corruption source and fixed severity: each period receives clean input
and the same Gaussian-noise, sensor-noise, blur, illumination, and other
predeclared individual transformations at severities 1, 2, and 3. This
separates the time effect from the corruption effect. A progressive
clean-to-noisy schedule may also be shown as an explicitly synthetic scenario,
but must not be used as evidence of naturally observed temporal drift. Keep
composite corruption source count fixed across severity. Save image panels
before training and use the same image IDs and deterministic corruption seeds
across model arms. Gaussian noise uses the first three ImageNet-C standard
deviations (0.08, 0.12, 0.18 on RGB values in [0, 1]); sensor noise and the
other acquisition changes retain their separately declared project levels.
For an explicit worsening trajectory, use clean historical, σ=0.08 adaptation,
and σ=0.12 future images; repeat with σ=0.18 in the future as a stronger
sensitivity condition. Crossed fixed-severity results remain the primary
synthetic comparison. Report Benjamini–Hochberg adjusted MMD tests and paired
score-difference intervals from a geographic-tile cluster bootstrap.
These interventions test synthetic covariate drift under the assumption that
the scene label remains valid; they do not establish natural temporal drift or
simulate changes to class priors or label definitions.

FILE: docs/external_dataset_evidence_plan.md

# External data for a balanced saliency claim

Select datasets before inspecting new outcomes. Report every named domain and
seed, including losses. The existing frozen-head screen finds a small CIFAR-10.1
gain (+0.30 ± 0.11 macro-F1 points across three seeds), but Yearbook (-0.33,
one seed) and Camelyon17 (-0.07 ± 0.12, three seeds) do not corroborate a
general advantage. These screen results test a head over frozen features, not
the full adapted router.

| Dataset and source | What it tests | Limitation and planned use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [CIFAR-10](https://cave.cs.toronto.edu/kriz/cifar.html) and [CIFAR-10.1 v6](https://github.com/modestyachts/CIFAR-10.1) | Independent recollection with the same ten classes; already gives the modest positive head result | Recollection is not a longitudinal update stream. Preserve all three existing seeds and report the small effect beside clean accuracy. |
| [CIFAR-10-C](https://zenodo.org/records/2535967) | Fixed common-corruption test over named sources and five severities | Synthetic corruptions are not calendar drift. Evaluate every source/severity with the same frozen checkpoint and no test-label tuning; report gain and harm counts. |
| [CIFAR-100](https://cave.cs.toronto.edu/kriz/cifar.html) and [CIFAR-100-C](https://zenodo.org/records/3555552) | Hundred-class stress test closer to CLEAR's label count | Still 32-pixel images and synthetic shift. Upscaling to the DINOv2 input size may blur patch-level interpretation; disclose it. |
| [Wild-Time Yearbook](https://wild-time.github.io/) | A genuine temporal counterexample already in the paper | One existing seed and frozen heads only. Repeat with matched seeds before inferring a temporal advantage. |
| [CIFAR-10-Warehouse](https://github.com/sxzrt/CIFAR-10-W) | Broader domain coverage if resources allow | Search-engine and generated-image domains can introduce selection and label-quality differences. Preselect domains without looking at model results. |

## Minimum fair protocol

1. On each dataset, fit uniform and saliency heads with the same backbone,
   images, split, seeds, epochs, optimiser schedule, and checkpoint rule.
2. Keep separate claims for the frozen-head screen and a full historical,
   updated, calibrated-router experiment. CIFAR-10.1 and CIFAR-C do not by
   themselves support real temporal-drift correction.
3. Report macro F1, clean score, per-domain scores, seed spread, and paired
   uncertainty. For routing also report always-historical, always-updated,
   confidence-only, saliency-only, combined, coverage, harmful switches, and
   missed beneficial updates.
4. Advance the narrow claim only if a predeclared aggregate improves without
   unacceptable clean degradation; preserve contrary domains in the same table.

The downloads and protocol should cite the original dataset sources above.
The corrected CLEAR conventional arm in `deploy/run_corrected_finetune_20261031.sh`
is the priority before adding more external experiments.
