FILE: docs/fmow_saliency_layer_pilot.md
================================================
# Saliency-guided layer-selection pilot

This is an exploratory architecture test on the custom FMoW-RGB chronological
subset. It does not replace the full drift protocol or establish covariate drift.

## Question

Can a frozen historical model's patch evidence guide **where** to adapt under
an unlabeled target-window shift, improving future macro-F1 over a feature-only
choice and a shuffled-evidence choice?

## Model and data flow

- DINOv2-small provides 12 blocks of patch tokens. A 62-class linear head is
  fitted to the mean of final patch tokens from 2010-13 `hist_train`, with
  cluster-disjoint `hist_val` for stopping. The head remains frozen afterward.
- The historical model's predicted-class patch contribution is
  `W[predicted_class] dot final_patch_token / patch_count`. Its sum plus bias
  must reconstruct that class's logit. The map explains the linear head's
  output; it is not an explanation of every backbone operation.
- Historical training images and unlabeled `future_a` images yield per-block
  patch tokens. Top-40% evidence weights pool each block's tokens. RBF MMD²
  compares historical and target pooled features. The E, M, or L group with
  the highest mean block MMD² is selected before adaptation or test scoring.
- Uniform patch weights and spatially shuffled, equal-area evidence weights
  produce separate control selections using the same images and MMD code.
- E (blocks 1-4), M (5-8), L (9-12), and ALL are trained separately from the
  same frozen model and head. Adapters update attention query/key/value,
  attention projection, and MLP fc1/fc2; the chosen blocks' LayerNorm affine
  parameters also update. E, M, and L must have equal trainable parameter
  counts. The target loss is prediction entropy minus batch-mean prediction
  entropy, using `future_a` without its labels. A0 is frozen inference.

## Readout

The primary pilot score is macro-F1 on cluster-disjoint `future_test`, over all
62 classes. Record accuracy and balanced accuracy too. The diagnostic is
useful only if its chosen group's score beats the uniform and shuffled choices
across meaningful replicated runs. If all selectors choose the same group,
this run cannot isolate a saliency-specific benefit. The best E/M/L arm is
reported after evaluation as an oracle comparison, never used to choose the
diagnostic. No hyperparameter or group choice uses `future_test` labels.

The 128-image-per-period server smoke test checks execution only. Its scores
are not evidence for the research question. A full run needs seeds 42, 43,
and 44, clustered uncertainty, natural-drift harm assessment, and controlled
shifts before any production-oriented conclusion.

Run one exploratory full seed with:

```bash
PYTHONPATH=src python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_saliency_layer_pilot \
  --manifest data/fmow_v1.1/subset_7k_country_matched/manifest_downloaded.csv \
  --output runs/fmow-saliency-layer-seed42 --seed 42
```

FILE: docs/fmow_v2.md

# fMoW v2 experiments

Code for the paper *Saliency-Guided Attention on a Frozen Vision Transformer for Temporal Adaptation under
Cloud Cover*. The base study lives in `src/fyp_drift/evaluation/` (`fmow_dinov2l_study.py`,
`saliency_attention_study.py`, `soft_saliency_heads.py`, `saliency_router_study.py`, ...). The v2 additions
are in `src/fyp_drift/evaluation/fmow_v2/`.

## Setup

```bash
source scripts/fmow_v2/env.sh   # paths for data, caches and outputs (edit for your machine)
```

## Run order

| Step | Command | Output |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Build the 14k-per-period subset | `python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.expand_subset` | manifest + images |
| 2. Drift, harm gate, cloud calibration, historical head | `python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_dinov2l_study --output $FYP_RUNS/main --stages gate calib` | `main/` |
| 3. Token caches (clean, then cloud) and 2x2 pooling | `saliency_adapters` (clean), `fmow_v2.tokens cloud`, `fmow_v2.tokens pool` | `$FYP_TOKENS` |
| 4. Attention and saliency heads | `saliency_attention_study`, `soft_saliency_heads` | `saliency_attention/`, `saliency_soft/` |
| 5. Parameter sweep and cloud-aware pooling | `fmow_v2.cloud_pooling` | `cloud_param/` |
| 6. Moved-object test, saliency in training | `fmow_v2.shift_test`, `fmow_v2.saliency_learning` | `shift_test/`, `saliency_learning/` |
| 7. Router | `saliency_router_study` | `saliency_router/` |
| 8. Held-out cloud tokens | `fmow_v2.tokens heldout` | `$FYP_TOKENS_EXTRA` |
| 9. Equal-training LoRA and hybrid | `scripts/fmow_v2/run_equal_training.sh` | `lora_fair/`, `hybrid/` |
| 10. Analysis and explanations | `fmow_v2.analysis`, `fmow_v2.explain` | `analysis/` |

Heads on held-out clouds reuse `saliency_attention_study --tokens DIR`, where `DIR` links the clean training
caches and the held-out test cache under the expected names.

## Protocol notes

- **Equal training.** Heads train for 15 epochs and LoRA/hybrid for up to 5, each keeping the best 2014–15
  validation epoch; LoRA stops after 2 epochs without improvement. LoRA's learning rate (3e-4) was chosen
  once on rank-2, clean, seed 42 from {1e-4, 3e-4}; heads use 1e-3.
- **LoRA sites.** q, k, v, attention output and both MLP layers of the last `--blocks` blocks
  (18,432 parameters per block per rank).
- **Losses.** The historical head uses class-weighted cross-entropy; all later heads and LoRA use plain
  cross-entropy.
- **Seeds.** Heads: 42, 43, 44. LoRA and hybrid: 42 first, 43 and 44 added afterwards.

## Outputs

- `results/fmow_v2/run_outputs/`: saved output of every run (per-image predictions); `results/fmow_v2/summary_tables.md` has the headline numbers.
- `figures/fmow_v2/`: every figure, the code that draws it (`plots/`) and its input data (`plots/data/`). Rebuild with `bash figures/fmow_v2/plots/make_all.sh`.

FILE: docs/FYP-execution-plan-refined.md
================================================
# FYP execution plan: test the saliency mechanism

## Material Passport

- Origin: refinement of the supplied `FYP-execution-plan.md` against the current checkout and the 31 October 2026 validation records
- Mode: experiment plan
- Date: 1 November 2026
- Verification status: plan updated from recorded results; the new server pilot and factorial results are not verified here
- Version: mechanism_plan_v2

This plan addresses the central question: **when image capture or context changes while the class still means the same thing, does learned patch weighting preserve task-relevant evidence better than ordinary updating?** The proposed benefit is specific. If the model can put more weight on stable object evidence and less on a changed background or local nuisance, its updated predictions may degrade less under that shift. A visible saliency map alone does not establish this benefit. The map must affect predictions, and a matched model without learned weighting must perform worse on the relevant conditions. Routing is a secondary extension after the classifier mechanism and utility have been established.

## Current evidence and claim boundary

The corrected 2011 split has already been used for a seed 42 server validation. The updated saliency model scored 80.78% macro F1 on the 2014 evidence-ranked mixture, against 80.81% without auxiliary saliency losses; routed scores were 79.75% and 79.60%. A corrected conventional fine-tuning arm scored 81.55% on that mixture. It beat the saliency update in nine of eleven 2014 conditions, tied in one, and lost in one. That strategy comparison starts from different historical heads, so it does not isolate the patch gate's effect. The fixed-checkpoint router-feature ablation has also been completed: at equal 50% disagreement coverage, confidence-only scored 79.98% and combined scored 79.74%. These are single-seed, already inspected results, not a fresh confirmatory test. See `paper/argument_audit_20261031.md` and `results/validation-20261031/` for the records.

The earlier plan's policy-replay implementation task is therefore complete and must not be presented as future work. The **seed 42 shared-checkpoint, patch-only factorial has now trained all four model arms**: on the 2014 evidence-ranked mixture, uniform updating and gated updating with auxiliary losses both score 81.81% macro F1; gating without auxiliary losses scores 81.74%. The script exited after training because secondary router replay found two mismatches among 164,945 rounded future-feature rows. A full fixed-checkpoint intervention on 4,999 clean and 4,999 mixture images found that uniform, permuted, and inverted weights changed only 0.14%, 0.14%, and 0.34% of mixture top-1 predictions. None lowered mixture macro F1. See `results/validation-20261101/`. This single-seed, already inspected evidence does not show a saliency accuracy advantage; seeds 43 and 44 and source-specific checks remain open.

## Causal chain and tests

| Link in the argument | Required observation | Competing explanation or boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The incoming images change | Detect distribution change on fixed-size, label-free image samples; separately measure task loss | Detectable feature change need not damage classification. Clean temporal change has not yet been detected reliably. |
| The change disrupts context more than the class evidence | Specify the nuisance, severity, and stable label assumption before scoring | Strong blur and compression may destroy the object evidence itself. |
| Learned weighting influences the classifier | Replace learned weights with uniform and permuted weights at fixed checkpoint; compare logits and predictions. Train a patch-only head that removes the direct class-token-to-head path. | Visually plausible maps and deletion panels do not prove prediction dependence. Replacement can create an off-distribution internal state. |
| Saliency improves adaptation | Compare uniform, gated without auxiliary losses, and gated with auxiliary losses from the same historical checkpoint, data, seed, tuning budget, and selection split | Robustness augmentation, model capacity, or checkpoint differences could explain an apparent gain. |
| Routing adds decision value | Replay confidence-only, map-only, and combined policies on the same frozen model pair and calibration/evaluation IDs; compare with always-updated at equal route coverage and declared error costs | Better explanations or fewer harmful routes alone do not imply better macro F1 or net utility. |

The likely benefit should be tested first on localized context or framing changes, including viewpoint and modest occlusion. Report global degradations such as blur, noise, and JPEG compression as separate stressors. A gain under those conditions may be useful, but should not automatically be attributed to spatial saliency. The method does not address changed label definitions, unseen classes, severe loss of object information, or annotation errors.

## Study sequence

1. **Freeze the next protocol.** Record the server checkout commit, hashes of uncommitted helper scripts, lockfile and CLEAR manifest, seeds, exact commands, named conditions, metrics, and selection rules. Preserve the 2004–2008 train / 2009 select / 2010 adapt / disjoint 2011 select and calibrate / 2012–2014 evaluate chronology. Report all eleven declared conditions, not just the evidence-ranked mixture. Label all 2012–2014 analyses as corrected-protocol replications or exploratory follow-ups because those data have been inspected.
2. **Reconcile the completed seed 42 server run.** The four training arms and full clean/mixture weight-swap diagnostic are complete. Verify the common historical checkpoint hash, uniform gate initialization, disjoint 2011 IDs, identical future IDs, and all source-specific scores from saved artifacts. The earlier script's dependence on another progress log and three-epoch historical budget must be reviewed against the frozen protocol before repeating it. The router replay's two rounded-feature mismatches are a secondary logging issue; preserve the failure record.
3. **Complete the saliency evidence table.** Run the fixed-checkpoint learned/uniform/permuted/inverted intervention for every declared 2014 source on the seed 42 checkpoint. Save prediction flips, logit changes, accuracy, and macro F1. Compare those results with the separately trained uniform and gated arms, keeping the fixed-checkpoint and training comparisons distinct. Record checkpoint, configuration, source-code, output, and sample-ID hashes.
4. **Repeat matched seeds.** Repeat the frozen factorial for seeds 43 and 44. Keep the same starting checkpoint *within each seed* and the same adaptation budget across arms. Do not tune the gate, thresholds, or chosen corruption subset on future labels. Report per-seed paired image intervals and between-seed spread separately.
5. **Revise the paper around the saliency result.** Put the research problem, hypothesized patch-weight mechanism, fixed-checkpoint intervention, and shared-checkpoint comparison before the router. Show the complete condition table, negative results, and provenance. Keep archived shared-calibration scores separate from corrected runs. The supplied `FYP report framing plan.docx` is a writing and test guide, but its positive 1.20-point comparison mixes split protocols and is superseded by the corrected single-seed conventional result; do not carry that figure into a current headline claim.
6. **Test routing as a secondary extension.** For each frozen model pair, compare always-historical, always-updated, confidence-only, map-only, and combined routing. Match route coverage or use a predeclared streaming threshold. Report macro F1, coverage, helpful/harmful/missed switches, classwise effects, and a prespecified cost sensitivity. A quota set from the full future batch is a transductive diagnostic, not a deployable streaming policy.

## Run integrity checks

The saved seed 42 factorial is ready for interpretation only after confirming that all four model paths finished on the intended hardware, the three updated arms share the exact historical starting weights, 2011 selection and calibration IDs do not overlap, evaluation IDs match across arms, output files parse, and runtime/storage estimates come from logs. If any check fails, fix the cause, version the protocol, and rerun the affected stage before repeating the factorial. The 500-image pilot accuracy was diagnostic and was not used to choose an arm or condition.

## Saliency code and test checks

- The fixed-checkpoint intervention now includes learned, uniform, permuted, and **inverted raw-gate** weights. Inversion uses `1 - sigmoid(gate_logit)` followed by normalization, matching the model's complementary-patch definition. Check prediction flips, true-class logit changes, accuracy, and macro F1 by condition. An intervention tests model dependence on the weights; it does not alone prove a training benefit.
- Test that all four interventions use the same backbone tokens and classifier weights, that inverse weights are normalized correctly, and that a direct class-token-only head masks changes to the pooled patch representation. Keep a trained patch-only comparison to address that bypass.
- In the server pilot, assert that uniform and gated patch-only models start with identical logits from the shared historical uniform checkpoint, and that changing the saved checkpoint or 2011 split fails early on resume. Compare the complete output schemas and sample IDs before interpreting any score.
- The report-framing document's disagreement-only router fit and random-coverage checks remain secondary. They cannot repair a missing saliency classifier effect and should follow the shared-checkpoint result.

## Interpretation rule

Claim a **bounded saliency-guided adaptation benefit** only if the shared-checkpoint comparison shows a repeatable gain over uniform updating in predeclared relevant conditions, the learned gate materially changes predictions, and the gain survives the no-auxiliary comparison. If the gate changes predictions but does not improve task performance, report a mechanism without a demonstrated utility gain. If the map does not materially affect predictions, describe it as an inspectable signal rather than a proven cause of robustness. Claim routing benefit only where it beats always-updated or a declared cost criterion; its audit trail is a separate contribution.

The 1 November 2026 server pilot and full clean/mixture fixed-checkpoint diagnostic are recorded in `results/validation-20261101/`. The multi-seed factorial and remaining source-specific interventions are still proposed work.

FILE: docs/getting_started.md

# Getting started

## 1. Install

```bash
git clone https://github.com/Rikhil-6/FYP.git && cd FYP
python -m venv .venv && source .venv/bin/activate     # Python 3.11
pip install -e .                                       # installs fyp_drift and its dependencies
pip install seaborn scipy safetensors                  # figures and saved models
```

A GPU is needed for training (a 16 GB Tesla T4 was used). Inference with a saved model runs on CPU.

## 2. Get the data

The experiments use **fMoW-RGB** (Functional Map of the World), which is public on AWS
(`s3://spacenet-dataset/Hosted-Datasets/fmow/fmow-rgb/`, no account needed).

```bash
source scripts/fmow_v2/env.sh                          # edit the paths at the top for your machine
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.expand_subset   # country-matched subset, ~14k images per period
python -m fyp_drift.data.fmow_metadata                 # per-image sun angle, cloud cover, off-nadir
```

`expand_subset` downloads only the images in the subset (about 42k JPEGs) plus their JSON metadata and writes
`manifest_downloaded.csv`. See [data.md](data.md) for the periods, splits and file layout.

## 3. Run the experiments

The full order is in [fmow_v2.md](fmow_v2.md). The short version:

```bash
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_dinov2l_study --output $FYP_RUNS/main      # drift, harm gate, cloud calibration
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.saliency_attention_study --run $FYP_RUNS/main   # attention and saliency heads
bash scripts/fmow_v2/run_equal_training.sh                                       # LoRA and the hybrid, 3 seeds
```

## 4. Rebuild the figures

```bash
bash figures/fmow_v2/plots/make_all.sh
```

## 5. Use a trained model

```bash
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.models predict models/fmow_v2/soft_gate my_site.jpg --map attention.png
```

See [models.md](models.md).

# Trained models

Saved in `models/fmow_v2/` as **safetensors** (weights only: safe to load, no pickle code execution, memory-mapped
and fast) plus a `config.json` with the class names, architecture and validation score. The frozen DINOv2-L
backbone is not stored; it downloads from the Hugging Face hub on first use (`facebook/dinov2-large`).

| Folder | Model | Trainable params | Clean test macro-F1 (seed 42) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `historical/` | Frozen 2010–13 linear head | – | 49.1 |
| `attention/` | Learned attention head | 196,929 | 59.9 |
| `hard_gate/` | Hard saliency gate | 196,931 | 59.4 |
| `soft_gate/` | Soft saliency gate | 196,932 | 60.4 |
| `lora_r2/` | LoRA rank 2, 6 blocks (adapters only) | 221,184 | ≈62.7 |
| `hybrid/` | LoRA r1, last 2 blocks + soft gate | 47,124 | ≈60.0 |

## Inference

```bash
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.models predict models/fmow_v2/soft_gate site.jpg
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.models predict models/fmow_v2/soft_gate site.jpg --box 0.52,0.48,0.30,0.22 --map attention.png
```

`--box` (object centre x, y, width, height as fractions of the image) crops the image the way the models were
trained; without it the whole image is used. `--map` saves the head's 10 x 10 attention over the image.

From Python:

```python
from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.models import Predictor

predictor = Predictor.load("models/fmow_v2/soft_gate")   # loads backbone + head once
result = predictor.predict("site.jpg")                   # {"top": [(class, prob), ...], "weights": 10x10 array}
```

Loading takes a few seconds (mostly the backbone); each image then takes well under a second on a GPU.

## Re-exporting

```bash
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.models export --out models/fmow_v2                     # heads, ~1 min each
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.equal_lora --rank 2 --seed 42 --export models/fmow_v2/lora_r2
python -m fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2.hybrid --seed 42 --export models/fmow_v2/hybrid
```



================================================
FILE: docs/saliency_architecture_followup.md
================================================
# Saliency as a drift-correction mechanism

The question is whether an adaptation method uses saliency to preserve task-relevant visual evidence when acquisition conditions change, beyond what ordinary fine-tuning or a generic augmentation would do. The router is a downstream option and is not needed to test this mechanism.

## Literature and design choices

- [Ma et al., *Rectify ViT Shortcut Learning by Visual Saliency*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2206.08567) restrict patch self-attention to salient patches while retaining a global residual. Our existing post-backbone saliency gate is weaker: it changes pooling after representations have formed. The learned-gate weight-swap test found virtually no causal contribution, so a new attempt should intervene **inside** representation learning, with a global path retained.
- [Ismail et al., *Improving Deep Learning Interpretability by Saliency Guided Training*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2111.14338) mask low-saliency features and encourage output agreement. This motivates the implemented saliency-mask LoRA training arm. The current pilot uses supervised CE on the masked view; an output-consistency term against the unmasked prediction is a distinct next ablation. The literature reports interpretability while preserving performance, not a guarantee of drift correction.
- [Zheng et al., *Instance-Warp*](https://arxiv.org/abs/2403.12712) use saliency to emphasize foreground objects during domain adaptation. This suggests a spatially local adaptation mechanism when camera background, lighting or hospital acquisition changes. For classification, a bounded patch reweighting or saliency-aware crop can be compared against a random crop with the same area and training budget.

## Test order

1. Verify the target window has distribution change and **historical-model harm**: clean split-half null, MMD² and permutation p, domain-classifier AUC, and paired prediction loss. Separate natural chronology from an injected perturbation.
2. Compare ordinary LoRA, random equal-area masks and saliency masks with identical backbone, trainable rank, IDs, seeds, epochs and selection rule. Keep all future labels out of adaptation and model selection. Report clean and shifted future performance together.
3. If masking consistently helps, test output-consistency training and a patch-level residual intervention separately. For a residual, preserve the global path and compare learned saliency, shuffled maps and uniform maps while holding parameter count fixed.
4. Repeat on a real chronological time × camera or time × acquisition dataset. A gain on synthetic occlusion alone only establishes mechanism sensitivity.

The current Yearbook result is selective: saliency-mask LoRA beats random masking under center occlusion in seeds 42–44 by 0.30–1.49 macro-F1 points (mean +0.90), but trails it under blur and usually noise, and trails ordinary LoRA on clean images. It beats ordinary LoRA under center occlusion in only two of three seeds. Thus the supported statement is an exploratory robustness signal for **one** injected occlusion condition on a later dated image set, not correction of the natural Yearbook drift. The exact numbers and drift tests are in `results/validation-20261102/yearbook-saliency-mask/`.



================================================
FILE: docs/saliency_lora_drift_protocol.md
================================================
# Saliency guided LoRA drift correction protocol

## Question and scope

Does a fixed historical class-evidence saliency map improve low-rank adaptation under acquisition shift beyond ordinary LoRA and a spatially uniform consistency loss? The saliency term is applied during adaptation. At deployment, all variants use the same patch-only ViT classifier; the map is not required for inference and no router is involved.

This is a test of correction after a labelled 2010 adaptation sample becomes available. The project has not established correction of naturally occurring year-to-year drift. Its controlled image transformations are synthetic acquisition stressors on a temporal CLEAR stream.

## Temporal split and model control

| Stage | Data | Permitted use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Historical training | 2004–2008 train-source buckets | Train the common uniform patch-only DINOv2 model. |
| Historical selection | 2009 | Select the frozen starting checkpoint. |
| Adaptation | 2010 train-source bucket | Train LoRA and classifier on the same labelled clean/shifted image pairs in every arm. The clean view exists because the shift is synthetically generated; this assumption does not hold for arbitrary real-world drift. |
| Updated selection | A class-stratified half of 2011 train-source | Select epoch and any development hyperparameters without using future labels. The other half is reserved and not used by this classifier experiment. |
| Frozen evaluation | 2012–2014 test-source buckets | Report clean and each named shift on identical image IDs. The 2014 severity-three mixture is a stress test of known source families. |

CLEAR's year labels are upload-stream buckets. The pilot uses only seed 42 and limits adaptation, selection, and evaluation samples. Those scores are exploratory. The 2014 conditions were examined before this method was proposed, so even a later full run on them remains a follow-up, not a fresh blind test.

## Arms and mechanism

Every arm starts from the same historical checkpoint, trains rank-four query/value LoRA in the last three ViT blocks plus the same classifier, and sees the same paired views, classification losses, optimizer settings, epoch budget, and sample IDs. The arms are:

1. Ordinary LoRA with classification losses on both views.
2. LoRA plus uniform patch-feature consistency.
3. LoRA plus consistency weighted by a shuffled saliency map.
4. LoRA plus consistency weighted by the true-class top-20% patch contributions of the frozen historical classifier on the clean *training* view.

The comparison of arm 4 with arm 2 asks whether spatial weighting helps beyond consistency itself. Arm 3 tests whether any spatially sparse weighting would suffice. The historical teacher is frozen and labels are used only in the adaptation stream. The objective encourages the adapted features at selected patches to remain stable between paired views; it does not prove the selected patches depict the class object. A separate map audit is required for that interpretation.

## Evaluation and interpretation

For each frozen checkpoint, use the same image IDs in the clean and shifted conditions. Report macro F1, accuracy, shift penalty relative to that arm's same-year clean score, and paired image uncertainty. Show source-specific results and the between-seed spread for seeds 42, 43, and 44. A future experiment should hold out one source family from 2010 augmentation and evaluate it unchanged in later buckets; the current pilot does not do that. Geometric transforms need spatial alignment before patchwise consistency is applied to their paired features.

Call the result a saliency-specific benefit only if the saliency arm repeatedly exceeds ordinary LoRA, uniform consistency, and shuffled-map consistency under a declared shift without a material clean-score loss. If all arms tie, or saliency and shuffled maps tie, report no identified contribution from the map. Do not select a favorable scenario or consistency weight using 2012–2014 labels.

The pilot implementation is `src/fyp_drift/training/saliency_lora_pilot.py`. The first 600-image, one-epoch run used a conservative LoRA learning rate and produced identical predictions across arms. A second 1,000-image, two-epoch run uses a corrected LoRA learning rate and records the separate loss terms. Both pilots are retained as exploratory diagnostics.

# Ubuntu server setup

The deployment scripts assume an Ubuntu checkout and a mounted data volume. By
default the repository stays under the user's home directory while large files
are kept under `/mnt/sdd/FYP`.

## Initial setup

```bash
git clone https://github.com/Rikhil-6/FYP.git
cd FYP
git switch Rik/transparent-router
bash deploy/remote_setup.sh
```

The setup script installs uv inside the storage directory, creates the locked
environment, and links the following paths:

```text
data   -> /mnt/sdd/FYP/data
runs   -> /mnt/sdd/FYP/runs
tools  -> /mnt/sdd/FYP/tools
.venv  -> /mnt/sdd/FYP/venv
cache  -> /mnt/sdd/FYP/cache
```

Existing directories are not silently replaced. Set `FYP_STORAGE_DIR` and the
individual `FYP_*_DIR` variables when a second checkout needs separate runs or
an environment while sharing the dataset and package cache.

## Checks

```bash
bash deploy/server.sh status
bash deploy/server.sh smoke
```

`status` prints the Git revision, PyTorch build, CUDA visibility, GPU model, CPU
count, and resolved storage links. `smoke` performs a small DINOv2 forward and
backward pass without starting a training run.

Full training requires a visible CUDA GPU unless `FYP_ALLOW_CPU_TRAIN=1` is set
explicitly. The launcher also checks that the installed PyTorch build agrees
with the lock file.

## Logged runs

Data ingestion and training remain separate commands:

```bash
bash deploy/server.sh ingest
bash deploy/launch.sh --detach train --temporal-repeats 1 --epochs 5 --batch-size 8
```

The detached launcher creates a tmux session and writes its terminal log under
`runs/pipelines/<timestamp>/`. Model-specific configurations, epoch events,
metrics, predictions, and checkpoints stay within their own run directories.

Useful tmux commands are:

```bash
tmux ls
tmux attach -t <session-name>
```

Detach with `Ctrl+B`, followed by `D`.

## Updating a private server checkout

The server does not require a GitHub credential. A local machine can create a
Git bundle, transfer it over SSH, and fetch the named branch from that bundle.
Private keys must remain outside the repository and are excluded by `.gitignore`.

FILE: docs/temporal_image_dataset_shortlist.md
================================================
# Image datasets for temporal drift correction

The study needs a chronological train → adaptation → future-test split **and** a measured performance loss. A significant MMD alone is insufficient. Each candidate below needs a clean-period reference, a null split-half control, MMD permutation test, domain-classifier AUC, and frozen historical-model accuracy before a correction claim is made. Patient/sequence/site IDs must stay disjoint where applicable; class composition and acquisition metadata need reporting by period.

| Candidate | Time and acquisition axes | Access and current project status | Main limitation / next test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [iWildCam-WILDS](https://github.com/p-lambda/wilds/blob/main/wilds/datasets/iwildcam_dataset.py) | Individual image datetime **and** camera location are exposed in WILDS metadata. | Public WILDS download, 11.96 GB compressed; server folder currently empty. | Best time × camera candidate. Custom chronological split within locations, plus held-out cameras, needed because official split is by camera. Check enough species support in each window before training. |
| [FMoW-WILDS](https://wilds.stanford.edu/datasets/) | Satellite imagery has time period and region metadata. | Public WILDS loader; server folder currently empty. | Strong real chronological imaging case, but large archive and geography/time confounding. Verify year and class overlap and download feasibility. |
| [PadChest](https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.07441) | Chest radiographs from 2009–2017, with acquisition information. | Public dataset described by authors; not downloaded. | Medical time × acquisition setting, but only one hospital. Use patient-disjoint chronology; verify dates, device metadata and access before promising a run. Report label-quality issues because many labels are automatic. |
| [Wild-Time Yearbook](https://wild-time.github.io/) | Image year 1930–2013. | Already present on server and trialled on GPU. | Fast chronology check, but low-resolution binary portrait task and no hospital/camera axis. Our current sampled split has distribution change but no natural performance harm. |

[Camelyon17-WILDS](https://wilds.stanford.edu/datasets/) is a useful hospital acquisition control, but its public WILDS setup exposes hospitals rather than a defensible chronological split. It must not be presented as temporal drift.

## Recommended next dataset

iWildCam is the most direct test of the requested joint **time × camera** question because its loader explicitly parses datetime and location. Before model fitting, inspect counts by year, camera and species; freeze a split and keep all images from each burst/sequence together. Then run frozen-model MMD, AUC and performance loss. If the chronological loss is absent, move to a different window under a predeclared selection rule rather than optimizing the test window for a saliency gain.

#!/bin/bash
# Equal-training LoRA and the LoRA + soft-gate hybrid for seeds 42-44 (about 30 min per run on a T4).
set -u
source "$(dirname "$0")/env.sh"
M=fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_v2
for seed in 42 43 44; do
  $PY -m $M.equal_lora --rank 2 --blocks 6 --condition clean --seed $seed
  $PY -m $M.equal_lora --rank 8 --blocks 6 --condition clean --seed $seed
  $PY -m $M.equal_lora --rank 1 --blocks 2 --condition clean --seed $seed
  $PY -m $M.hybrid --seed $seed
  $PY -m $M.equal_lora --rank 2 --blocks 6 --condition cloud --seed $seed
  $PY -m $M.equal_lora --rank 8 --blocks 6 --condition cloud --seed $seed
done

# Pilot scripts

One-off scripts from the first day of the fMoW study (3 Nov 2026): data checks (`step0_check.py`), the first
supervised and shuffled-saliency checks, the study-B pilot and the one-day DINOv2-L run (`fmow_today.py`).
They are kept for the record; the maintained versions are in `src/fyp_drift/evaluation/`.



================================================
FILE: scripts/pilots/fmow_today.py
================================================
"""One-day FMoW study on DINOv2-L: harm gate, cloud calibration, matched anchoring arms.

Stages (each resumable; outputs under --output):
  cache    decode every manifest image once to a uint8 224x224 memmap
  features frozen DINOv2-L mean-patch + CLS features (fp16) for every image
  gate     linear head on historical train; scores by window; MMD / domain AUC
  calib    cloud alpha chosen on historical-val so the frozen drop is 5-10 points
  arms     LoRA (last K blocks) supervised adaptation: plain, uniform/shuffled/saliency anchors
           x lambda grid (picked on adapt-val) x condition (clean, cloud) x seeds
  summary  per-arm table + paired clustered bootstrap deltas

Future-test labels are used only for final scoring, never for any choice.
"""
from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import hashlib
import json
import math
import time
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import torch
from PIL import Image
from sklearn.metrics import accuracy_score, balanced_accuracy_score, f1_score
from sklearn.model_selection import GroupShuffleSplit
from torch import nn
from torch.nn import functional as F
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader, Dataset

Image.MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS = None
MEAN = torch.tensor([0.485, 0.456, 0.406])[:, None, None]
STD = torch.tensor([0.229, 0.224, 0.225])[:, None, None]
PERIODS = ("historical_2010_13", "adapt_2014_15", "future_2016_17")


def log(*parts):
    print(time.strftime("%H:%M:%S"), *parts, flush=True)


def dump(path, obj):
    path = Path(path)
    tmp = path.with_suffix(path.suffix + ".tmp")
    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(obj, indent=2, default=float))
    tmp.replace(path)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- data
def load_rows(manifest):
    with open(manifest, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
        rows = list(csv.DictReader(handle))
    rows = [r for r in rows if r.get("local_path") and Path(r["local_path"]).exists()]
    classes = sorted({r["category"] for r in rows})
    index = {c: i for i, c in enumerate(classes)}
    for i, r in enumerate(rows):
        r["class_id"] = index[r["category"]]
        r["idx"] = i
        r["group"] = f"{r['country']}:{r['utm']}"
    return rows, classes


def divide_groups(rows, fraction, seed):
    groups = [r["group"] for r in rows]
    a, b = next(GroupShuffleSplit(n_splits=1, test_size=fraction, random_state=seed)
                .split(rows, groups=groups))
    left, right = [rows[i] for i in a], [rows[i] for i in b]
    assert not ({r["group"] for r in left} & {r["group"] for r in right})
    return left, right


def make_splits(rows):
    by = {p: sorted((r for r in rows if r["period"] == p), key=lambda r: r["local_path"])
          for p in PERIODS}
    hist_train, hist_val = divide_groups(by[PERIODS[0]], 0.15, 42)
    adapt_train, adapt_val = divide_groups(by[PERIODS[1]], 0.1, 42)
    _, future_test = divide_groups(by[PERIODS[2]], 0.5, 43)   # same test half as study B
    return {"hist_train": hist_train, "hist_val": hist_val, "adapt_train": adapt_train,
            "adapt_val": adapt_val, "future_test": future_test, "future_all": by[PERIODS[2]]}


def build_cache(rows, path, workers):
    path = Path(path)
    done_flag = path.with_suffix(".done")
    if done_flag.exists():
        return np.load(path, mmap_mode="r")
    arr = np.lib.format.open_memmap(path, mode="w+", dtype=np.uint8, shape=(len(rows), 224, 224, 3))

    class Decode(Dataset):
        def __len__(self):
            return len(rows)

        def __getitem__(self, i):
            with Image.open(rows[i]["local_path"]) as im:
                im.draft("RGB", (448, 448))
                im = im.convert("RGB").resize((224, 224), Image.BILINEAR)
            return i, torch.from_numpy(np.asarray(im, dtype=np.uint8).copy())

    t = time.time()
    for n, (ids, ims) in enumerate(DataLoader(Decode(), batch_size=64, num_workers=workers)):
        arr[ids.numpy()] = ims.numpy()
        if n % 50 == 0:
            log(f"cache {int(ids[-1]) + 1}/{len(rows)} ({time.time() - t:.0f}s)")
    arr.flush()
    done_flag.write_text("ok")
    return np.load(path, mmap_mode="r")


def cloud_overlay(image, key, alpha, seed):
    """Fixed localized near-white overlay on a normalized tensor; same key -> same cloud."""
    digest = hashlib.sha256(f"{seed}:{key}".encode()).digest()
    gen = torch.Generator().manual_seed(int.from_bytes(digest[:8], "little"))
    noise = torch.rand((1, 1, 16, 16), generator=gen)
    smooth = F.avg_pool2d(noise, 5, stride=1, padding=2)
    mask = (smooth >= torch.quantile(smooth, 0.8)).float()
    mask = F.interpolate(mask, size=image.shape[-2:], mode="bilinear", align_corners=False)[0]
    white = ((1 - MEAN) / STD)
    return image * (1 - alpha * mask) + white * (alpha * mask)


class CachedImages(Dataset):
    def __init__(self, cache, rows, cloud_alpha=0.0, seed=42):
        self.cache, self.rows, self.alpha, self.seed = cache, rows, cloud_alpha, seed

    def __len__(self):
        return len(self.rows)

    def __getitem__(self, i):
        r = self.rows[i]
        x = torch.from_numpy(np.array(self.cache[r["idx"]])).permute(2, 0, 1).float() / 255.0
        x = (x - MEAN) / STD
        if self.alpha > 0:
            x = cloud_overlay(x, r["local_path"], self.alpha, self.seed)
        return x, r["class_id"], i


def loader(cache, rows, bs, workers, alpha=0.0, shuffle=False, seed=42):
    return DataLoader(CachedImages(cache, rows, alpha), batch_size=bs, shuffle=shuffle,
                      num_workers=workers, pin_memory=True, drop_last=shuffle,
                      generator=torch.Generator().manual_seed(seed) if shuffle else None)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- model
class LoRALinear(nn.Module):
    def __init__(self, base, rank=8, alpha=16):
        super().__init__()
        self.base = base
        self.down = nn.Linear(base.in_features, rank, bias=False)
        self.up = nn.Linear(rank, base.out_features, bias=False)
        nn.init.kaiming_uniform_(self.down.weight, a=math.sqrt(5))
        nn.init.zeros_(self.up.weight)
        self.scale = alpha / rank
        self.enabled = True
        for p in base.parameters():
            p.requires_grad = False

    def forward(self, x):
        out = self.base(x)
        if self.enabled:
            out = out + self.up(self.down(x)) * self.scale
        return out


class Backbone(nn.Module):
    """DINOv2 with frozen early blocks run under no_grad and LoRA on the last K blocks."""

    def __init__(self, name, trainable_blocks=0, rank=8):
        super().__init__()
        from transformers import Dinov2Model
        self.model = Dinov2Model.from_pretrained(name)
        for p in self.model.parameters():
            p.requires_grad = False
        self.width = self.model.config.hidden_size
        self.blocks = self.model.encoder.layer
        self.split = len(self.blocks) - trainable_blocks
        self.loras = []
        for block in list(self.blocks)[self.split:]:
            att = block.attention.attention
            for n in ("query", "key", "value"):
                m = LoRALinear(getattr(att, n), rank, 2 * rank)
                setattr(att, n, m)
                self.loras.append(m)
            for parent, n in ((block.attention.output, "dense"), (block.mlp, "fc1"), (block.mlp, "fc2")):
                m = LoRALinear(getattr(parent, n), rank, 2 * rank)
                setattr(parent, n, m)
                self.loras.append(m)

    def set_lora(self, on):
        for m in self.loras:
            m.enabled = on

    def trainable(self):
        return [p for p in self.parameters() if p.requires_grad]

    @staticmethod
    def _run(block, h):
        out = block(h)
        return out[0] if isinstance(out, (tuple, list)) else out

    def stem(self, x):
        with torch.no_grad():
            h = self.model.embeddings(x)
            for block in list(self.blocks)[:self.split]:
                h = self._run(block, h)
        return h

    def tail(self, h):
        for block in list(self.blocks)[self.split:]:
            h = self._run(block, h)
        return self.model.layernorm(h)

    def forward(self, x):
        return self.tail(self.stem(x))


def metrics(labels, preds, classes):
    labels, preds = np.asarray(labels), np.asarray(preds)
    ids = np.arange(classes)
    counts = np.bincount(preds, minlength=classes)
    return {"samples": int(len(labels)), "accuracy": float(accuracy_score(labels, preds)),
            "macro_f1": float(f1_score(labels, preds, labels=ids, average="macro", zero_division=0)),
            "balanced_accuracy": float(balanced_accuracy_score(labels, preds)),
            "unique_predictions": int((counts > 0).sum()),
            "max_class_share": float(counts.max() / max(1, len(preds)))}


@torch.no_grad()
def evaluate(backbone, head, data, classes):
    backbone.eval()
    labels, preds = [], []
    for x, y, _ in data:
        with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.float16):
            tok = backbone(x.cuda(non_blocking=True))[:, 1:]
        preds.append(head(tok.float().mean(1)).argmax(1).cpu())
        labels.append(y)
    labels, preds = torch.cat(labels).numpy(), torch.cat(preds).numpy()
    return metrics(labels, preds, classes), preds


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- stages
def stage_features(args, cache, rows):
    out = args.output / "features.npz"
    if out.exists():
        return np.load(out)
    bb = Backbone(args.backbone).cuda().eval()
    data = loader(cache, rows, args.eval_batch, args.workers)
    means, clss = [], []
    t = time.time()
    with torch.no_grad():
        for n, (x, _, _) in enumerate(data):
            with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.float16):
                h = bb(x.cuda(non_blocking=True))
            means.append(h[:, 1:].float().mean(1).half().cpu())
            clss.append(h[:, 0].half().cpu())
            if n % 50 == 0:
                log(f"features {n * args.eval_batch}/{len(rows)} ({time.time() - t:.0f}s)")
    np.savez(out, mean=torch.cat(means).numpy(), cls=torch.cat(clss).numpy())
    log(f"features done: {len(rows) / (time.time() - t):.1f} img/s")
    del bb
    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
    return np.load(out)


def fit_head(x, y, classes, xv, yv, seed=42, epochs=300):
    torch.manual_seed(seed)
    x, xv = torch.tensor(x).float().cuda(), torch.tensor(xv).float().cuda()
    y, yv = torch.tensor(y).cuda(), torch.tensor(yv).cuda()
    counts = torch.bincount(y, minlength=classes).float().clamp_min(1)
    weight = (counts.sum() / (classes * counts))
    head = nn.Linear(x.shape[1], classes).cuda()
    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(head.parameters(), lr=1e-3, weight_decay=1e-4)
    best, best_state = -1, None
    for epoch in range(epochs):
        perm = torch.randperm(len(x), device="cuda")
        for i in range(0, len(x), 512):
            b = perm[i:i + 512]
            loss = F.cross_entropy(head(x[b]), y[b], weight=weight)
            opt.zero_grad()
            loss.backward()
            opt.step()
        if epoch % 10 == 9:
            with torch.no_grad():
                score = f1_score(yv.cpu(), head(xv).argmax(1).cpu(), average="macro", zero_division=0)
            if score > best:
                best, best_state = score, {k: v.clone() for k, v in head.state_dict().items()}
    head.load_state_dict(best_state)
    return head.eval(), best


def stage_gate(args, feats, splits, classes):
    out = args.output / "gate.json"
    if out.exists():
        return json.loads(out.read_text()), torch.load(args.output / "head.pt", weights_only=True)
    f = feats["mean"].astype(np.float32)
    ix = {k: np.array([r["idx"] for r in v]) for k, v in splits.items()}
    lab = {k: np.array([r["class_id"] for r in v]) for k, v in splits.items()}
    head, val_f1 = fit_head(f[ix["hist_train"]], lab["hist_train"], classes,
                            f[ix["hist_val"]], lab["hist_val"])
    torch.save(head.state_dict(), args.output / "head.pt")
    scores = {}
    with torch.no_grad():
        for k in ("hist_val", "adapt_val", "adapt_train", "future_test", "future_all"):
            pred = head(torch.tensor(f[ix[k]]).cuda()).argmax(1).cpu().numpy()
            scores[k] = metrics(lab[k], pred, classes)
    # drift statistics on frozen features, 1000 per group
    drift = []
    try:
        from fyp_drift.evaluation.drift_metrics import distribution_shift_metrics
        rng = np.random.default_rng(42)
        def pick(name, n):
            return f[rng.choice(ix[name], size=min(n, len(ix[name])), replace=False)]
        hist_all = np.concatenate([ix["hist_train"], ix["hist_val"]])
        hsel = f[rng.choice(hist_all, size=min(2000, len(hist_all)), replace=False)]
        pairs = {"historical_vs_future": (hsel[:1000], pick("future_all", 1000)),
                 "historical_vs_adapt": (hsel[:1000], f[rng.choice(np.concatenate([ix["adapt_train"], ix["adapt_val"]]), 1000, replace=False)]),
                 "historical_split_half_null": (hsel[:1000], hsel[1000:2000])}
        for name, (a, b) in pairs.items():
            m = distribution_shift_metrics(a, b, permutations=args.permutations, permutation_limit=1000, seed=42)
            drift.append({"comparison": name, **{k: v for k, v in m.items() if not isinstance(v, (list, dict))}})
            log("drift", name, drift[-1].get("mmd_squared"), drift[-1].get("mmd_permutation_p"),
                drift[-1].get("domain_classifier_roc_auc"))
    except Exception as exc:  # keep the gate result even if drift metrics fail
        drift.append({"error": repr(exc)})
    # clustered bootstrap: historical-val vs future-test accuracy gap (independent samples)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(0)
    def cluster_acc(rows_, preds_, labels_):
        g = np.array([r["group"] for r in rows_])
        u = np.unique(g)
        return g, u, (preds_ == labels_).astype(float)
    with torch.no_grad():
        ph = head(torch.tensor(f[ix["hist_val"]]).cuda()).argmax(1).cpu().numpy()
        pf = head(torch.tensor(f[ix["future_test"]]).cuda()).argmax(1).cpu().numpy()
    gh, uh, ch = cluster_acc(splits["hist_val"], ph, lab["hist_val"])
    gf, uf, cf = cluster_acc(splits["future_test"], pf, lab["future_test"])
    gaps = []
    for _ in range(1000):
        sh = rng.choice(uh, len(uh)); sf = rng.choice(uf, len(uf))
        a = np.concatenate([ch[gh == s] for s in sh]); b = np.concatenate([cf[gf == s] for s in sf])
        gaps.append(a.mean() - b.mean())
    gate = {"backbone": args.backbone, "head_hist_val_macro_f1": val_f1, "scores": scores, "drift": drift,
            "accuracy_gap_hist_val_minus_future": {"mean": float(np.mean(gaps)),
                                                    "ci95": [float(np.percentile(gaps, 2.5)), float(np.percentile(gaps, 97.5))]},
            "counts": {k: len(v) for k, v in splits.items()}}
    gap = gate["accuracy_gap_hist_val_minus_future"]
    gate["harm_gate_passed"] = bool(gap["mean"] >= 0.03 and gap["ci95"][0] > 0)
    dump(out, gate)
    return gate, head.state_dict()


def stage_calib(args, cache, splits, head_state, classes):
    out = args.output / "calib.json"
    if out.exists():
        return json.loads(out.read_text())
    bb = Backbone(args.backbone).cuda().eval()
    head = nn.Linear(bb.width, classes).cuda()
    head.load_state_dict(head_state)
    rows = []
    for a in (0.0, 0.2, 0.35, 0.5, 0.7, 0.85):
        m, _ = evaluate(bb, head, loader(cache, splits["hist_val"], args.eval_batch, args.workers, a), classes)
        rows.append({"alpha": a, **m})
        log("calib", a, m["macro_f1"])
    clean = rows[0]["macro_f1"]
    pick = min(rows[1:], key=lambda r: abs((clean - r["macro_f1"]) - 0.075))
    res = {"candidates": rows, "selected_alpha": pick["alpha"],
           "selected_drop": clean - pick["macro_f1"],
           "within_5_10_points": bool(0.05 <= clean - pick["macro_f1"] <= 0.10)}
    dump(out, res)
    del bb
    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
    return res


def anchor_weights(ref_tokens, head, labels, mode, gen):
    """Weights over patches summing to one. Saliency = true-class evidence of the frozen model."""
    n = ref_tokens.shape[1]
    if mode == "uniform":
        return torch.full(ref_tokens.shape[:2], 1.0 / n, device=ref_tokens.device)
    evidence = (ref_tokens * head.weight[labels][:, None, :]).sum(-1)
    k = round(0.4 * n)
    top = evidence.topk(k, dim=1).indices
    w = torch.zeros_like(evidence).scatter_(1, top, 1.0 / k)
    if mode == "shuffled":
        order = torch.rand(w.shape, generator=gen).argsort(1).to(w.device)
        w = w.gather(1, order)
    return w


def train_arm(args, cache, splits, head, classes, mode, lam, alpha, seed):
    torch.manual_seed(seed)
    np.random.seed(seed)
    bb = Backbone(args.backbone, args.trainable_blocks, args.rank).cuda()
    params = bb.trainable()
    opt = torch.optim.AdamW(params, lr=args.lr, weight_decay=0.01)
    scaler = torch.amp.GradScaler("cuda")
    gen = torch.Generator().manual_seed(seed + 7)
    # step-zero equivalence: adapters start at zero
    x0 = next(iter(loader(cache, splits["adapt_val"][:8], 8, 0, alpha)))[0].cuda()
    with torch.no_grad(), torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.float16):
        bb.set_lora(False); a0 = head(bb(x0)[:, 1:].float().mean(1))
        bb.set_lora(True); b0 = head(bb(x0)[:, 1:].float().mean(1))
    step0 = float((a0 - b0).abs().max())
    history = []
    accum = max(1, args.effective_batch // args.batch)
    for epoch in range(args.epochs):
        bb.train()
        data = loader(cache, splits["adapt_train"], args.batch, args.workers, alpha, shuffle=True, seed=seed + epoch)
        ce_sum = an_sum = n = 0
        opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        for step, (x, y, _) in enumerate(data):
            x, y = x.cuda(non_blocking=True), y.cuda(non_blocking=True)
            with torch.autocast("cuda", dtype=torch.float16):
                h = bb.stem(x)
                tok = bb.tail(h)[:, 1:].float()
                ce = F.cross_entropy(head(tok.mean(1)), y)
                anchor = torch.zeros((), device="cuda")
                if mode != "plain" and lam > 0:
                    with torch.no_grad():
                        bb.set_lora(False)
                        ref = bb.tail(h)[:, 1:].float()
                        bb.set_lora(True)
                        w = anchor_weights(ref, head, y, mode, gen)
                    anchor = (w * (tok - ref).square().mean(-1)).sum(1).mean()
                loss = (ce + lam * anchor) / accum
            scaler.scale(loss).backward()
            if (step + 1) % accum == 0:
                scaler.step(opt)
                scaler.update()
                opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
            ce_sum += float(ce); an_sum += float(anchor); n += 1
        val, _ = evaluate(bb, head, loader(cache, splits["adapt_val"], args.eval_batch, args.workers, alpha), classes)
        history.append({"epoch": epoch + 1, "ce": ce_sum / n, "anchor_mse": an_sum / n, "adapt_val": val})
        log(f"  {mode} lam={lam} a={alpha} s={seed} ep{epoch + 1} ce={ce_sum / n:.3f} anchor={an_sum / n:.4f} val_f1={val['macro_f1']:.4f}")
    test, preds = evaluate(bb, head, loader(cache, splits["future_test"], args.eval_batch, args.workers, alpha), classes)
    peak = torch.cuda.max_memory_allocated() / 2**30
    del bb, opt
    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
    torch.cuda.reset_peak_memory_stats()
    return {"mode": mode, "lambda": lam, "cloud_alpha": alpha, "seed": seed, "step0_max_logit_diff": step0,
            "trainable_parameters": int(sum(p.numel() for p in params)), "history": history,
            "adapt_val": history[-1]["adapt_val"], "future_test": test, "predictions": preds.tolist(),
            "peak_gpu_gib": peak}


def stage_arms(args, cache, splits, head_state, classes, alpha_cloud):
    head = nn.Linear(1024 if "large" in args.backbone else 768, classes).cuda().eval()
    head.load_state_dict(head_state)
    for p in head.parameters():
        p.requires_grad = False
    arms_dir = args.output / "arms"
    arms_dir.mkdir(exist_ok=True)
    conditions = {"clean": 0.0, "cloud": alpha_cloud}
    # frozen reference scores per condition (and regime A: clean model on cloudy test is the clean arm re-scored below)
    frozen = {}
    bb = Backbone(args.backbone).cuda().eval()
    for cond, a in conditions.items():
        p = arms_dir / f"frozen_{cond}.json"
        if not p.exists():
            m, preds = evaluate(bb, head, loader(cache, splits["future_test"], args.eval_batch, args.workers, a), classes)
            dump(p, {"mode": "frozen", "condition": cond, "future_test": m, "predictions": preds.tolist()})
        frozen[cond] = json.loads(p.read_text())["future_test"]
        log("frozen", cond, frozen[cond]["macro_f1"])
    del bb
    torch.cuda.empty_cache()
    for seed in args.seeds:
        for cond, a in conditions.items():
            for mode in ("plain", "uniform", "shuffled", "saliency"):
                lams = [0.0] if mode == "plain" else args.lambdas
                for lam in lams:
                    p = arms_dir / f"{cond}_{mode}_l{lam}_s{seed}.json"
                    if p.exists():
                        continue
                    t = time.time()
                    res = train_arm(args, cache, splits, head, classes, mode, lam, a, seed)
                    res["condition"] = cond
                    res["seconds"] = time.time() - t
                    dump(p, res)
                    log(f"DONE {p.name}: test_f1={res['future_test']['macro_f1']:.4f} val_f1={res['adapt_val']['macro_f1']:.4f} "
                        f"peak={res['peak_gpu_gib']:.1f}GiB {res['seconds'] / 60:.1f}min")


def cluster_boot(a_pred, b_pred, labels, groups, reps=2000, seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    diff = (a_pred == labels).astype(float) - (b_pred == labels).astype(float)
    u = np.unique(groups)
    by = {g: diff[groups == g] for g in u}
    stats = [np.concatenate([by[g] for g in rng.choice(u, len(u))]).mean() for _ in range(reps)]
    return {"delta_acc": float(diff.mean()), "ci95": [float(np.percentile(stats, 2.5)), float(np.percentile(stats, 97.5))]}


def stage_summary(args, splits, classes):
    arms_dir = args.output / "arms"
    labels = np.array([r["class_id"] for r in splits["future_test"]])
    groups = np.array([r["group"] for r in splits["future_test"]])
    results = [json.loads(p.read_text()) for p in sorted(arms_dir.glob("*.json"))]
    table, chosen = [], {}
    for r in results:
        table.append({"condition": r.get("condition"), "mode": r["mode"], "lambda": r.get("lambda"),
                      "seed": r.get("seed"), "adapt_val_f1": r.get("adapt_val", {}).get("macro_f1"),
                      **{f"test_{k}": r["future_test"][k] for k in ("macro_f1", "accuracy", "unique_predictions")}})
    # lambda chosen per (condition, mode) on mean adapt-val F1 across seeds (never test)
    for cond in ("clean", "cloud"):
        for mode in ("uniform", "shuffled", "saliency"):
            cands = {}
            for r in results:
                if r.get("condition") == cond and r["mode"] == mode:
                    cands.setdefault(r["lambda"], []).append(r["adapt_val"]["macro_f1"])
            if cands:
                chosen[(cond, mode)] = max(cands, key=lambda l: np.mean(cands[l]))
    def get(cond, mode, seed):
        lam = 0.0 if mode == "plain" else chosen.get((cond, mode))
        for r in results:
            if r.get("condition") == cond and r["mode"] == mode and r.get("seed") == seed and r.get("lambda") == lam:
                return r
    comparisons, means = [], {}
    for cond in ("clean", "cloud"):
        for mode in ("plain", "uniform", "shuffled", "saliency"):
            vals = [get(cond, mode, s)["future_test"]["macro_f1"] for s in args.seeds if get(cond, mode, s)]
            if vals:
                means[f"{cond}/{mode}"] = {"lambda": 0.0 if mode == "plain" else chosen.get((cond, mode)),
                                           "seeds": len(vals), "mean_test_macro_f1": float(np.mean(vals)),
                                           "sd": float(np.std(vals, ddof=1)) if len(vals) > 1 else None}
        for a, b in (("saliency", "uniform"), ("saliency", "shuffled"), ("saliency", "plain"), ("uniform", "plain")):
            for s in args.seeds:
                ra, rb = get(cond, a, s), get(cond, b, s)
                if ra and rb:
                    comparisons.append({"condition": cond, "a": a, "b": b, "seed": s,
                                        "delta_macro_f1": ra["future_test"]["macro_f1"] - rb["future_test"]["macro_f1"],
                                        **cluster_boot(np.array(ra["predictions"]), np.array(rb["predictions"]), labels, groups)})
    dump(args.output / "summary.json", {"chosen_lambda": {f"{k[0]}/{k[1]}": v for k, v in chosen.items()},
                                        "means": means, "paired": comparisons, "all_runs": table})
    with open(args.output / "summary.csv", "w", newline="") as h:
        w = csv.DictWriter(h, fieldnames=list(table[0].keys()))
        w.writeheader(); w.writerows(table)
    log("summary written", json.dumps(means, indent=1))


def main(argv=None):
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    ap.add_argument("--manifest", type=Path, default=Path("/mnt/sdd/FYP/data/fmow_v1.1/subset_7k_country_matched/manifest_downloaded.csv"))
    ap.add_argument("--output", type=Path, required=True)
    ap.add_argument("--backbone", default="facebook/dinov2-large")
    ap.add_argument("--stages", nargs="+", default=["cache", "features", "gate", "calib", "arms", "summary"])
    ap.add_argument("--workers", type=int, default=4)
    ap.add_argument("--eval-batch", type=int, default=64)
    ap.add_argument("--batch", type=int, default=16)
    ap.add_argument("--effective-batch", type=int, default=32)
    ap.add_argument("--epochs", type=int, default=2)
    ap.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=1e-4)
    ap.add_argument("--rank", type=int, default=8)
    ap.add_argument("--trainable-blocks", type=int, default=6)
    ap.add_argument("--lambdas", type=float, nargs="+", default=[1.0, 10.0])
    ap.add_argument("--seeds", type=int, nargs="+", default=[42, 43, 44])
    ap.add_argument("--permutations", type=int, default=499)
    ap.add_argument("--limit", type=int, default=0, help="smoke test: cap every split")
    args = ap.parse_args(argv)
    args.output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    dump(args.output / "config.json", {k: str(v) for k, v in vars(args).items()})
    rows, classes = load_rows(args.manifest)
    splits = make_splits(rows)
    if args.limit:
        splits = {k: v[:args.limit] for k, v in splits.items()}
    log("rows", len(rows), "classes", len(classes), {k: len(v) for k, v in splits.items()})
    cache = build_cache(rows, Path("/mnt/sdd/FYP/cache/fmow21k_u8_224.npy"), args.workers) if "cache" in args.stages or True else None
    feats = stage_features(args, cache, rows) if any(s in args.stages for s in ("features", "gate")) else None
    gate, head_state = stage_gate(args, feats, splits, len(classes)) if feats is not None else (None, None)
    if gate:
        log("GATE", json.dumps({k: gate[k] for k in ("harm_gate_passed", "accuracy_gap_hist_val_minus_future")}),
            {k: round(v["macro_f1"], 4) for k, v in gate["scores"].items()})
    calib = stage_calib(args, cache, splits, head_state, len(classes)) if "calib" in args.stages else None
    if "arms" in args.stages:
        stage_arms(args, cache, splits, head_state, len(classes), calib["selected_alpha"])
    if "summary" in args.stages:
        stage_summary(args, splits, len(classes))


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

import json
from pathlib import Path

import torch
from torch import nn
from transformers import Dinov2Model

from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_saliency_layer_pilot import divide_groups, loader
from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_temporal_pilot import load_manifest
from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_layer_selection import add_group_lora
from fyp_drift.models.vision_transformer import BACKBONE

run = Path('/mnt/sdd/FYP/runs/fmow-saliency-layer-full-seed42-20261103')
config = json.loads((run / 'config.json').read_text())
rows, categories = load_manifest(Path(config['manifest']))
future = sorted((row for row in rows if row['period'] == 'future_2016_17'), key=lambda row: row['local_path'])
_, test = divide_groups(future, 0.5, 43)
images, labels, _ = next(iter(loader(test[:8], 8, 0, True)))
images = images.cuda()
head = nn.Linear(384, len(categories)).cuda().eval()
head.load_state_dict(torch.load(run / 'head.pt', map_location='cuda', weights_only=True))
model = Dinov2Model.from_pretrained(BACKBONE).cuda().eval()
with torch.inference_mode(), torch.autocast('cuda'):
    before = head(model(pixel_values=images).last_hidden_state[:, 1:].mean(1).float())
for group in ('E', 'M', 'L', 'ALL'):
    wrapped = Dinov2Model.from_pretrained(BACKBONE).cuda().eval()
    add_group_lora(wrapped, group)
    wrapped.cuda().eval()
    with torch.inference_mode(), torch.autocast('cuda'):
        after = head(wrapped(pixel_values=images).last_hidden_state[:, 1:].mean(1).float())
    print(json.dumps({'group': group, 'max_logit_diff': (before-after).abs().max().item(),
                      'prediction_match': (before.argmax(1)==after.argmax(1)).sum().item(),
                      'images': len(images)}), flush=True)

"""One-seed matched supervised anchoring pilot. Future labels are evaluation only."""
import json
import time
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import torch
from torch import nn
from torch.utils.data import DataLoader
from tqdm import tqdm
from transformers import Dinov2Model

from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_saliency_layer_pilot import divide_groups, evaluate, loader
from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_temporal_pilot import FMoWImages, load_manifest
from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_layer_selection import add_group_lora
from fyp_drift.models.vision_transformer import BACKBONE

source = Path('/mnt/sdd/FYP/runs/fmow-saliency-layer-full-seed42-20261103')
output = Path('/mnt/sdd/FYP/runs/fmow-study-b-anchoring-seed42-20261103')
output.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
config = json.loads((source / 'config.json').read_text())
rows, categories = load_manifest(Path(config['manifest']))
adapt_rows = sorted((r for r in rows if r['period'] == 'adapt_2014_15'), key=lambda r: r['local_path'])
future = sorted((r for r in rows if r['period'] == 'future_2016_17'), key=lambda r: r['local_path'])
train, val = divide_groups(adapt_rows, 0.1, 42)
_, test = divide_groups(future, 0.5, 43)
torch.manual_seed(42)
head = nn.Linear(384, len(categories)).cuda().eval()
head.load_state_dict(torch.load(source / 'head.pt', map_location='cuda', weights_only=True))
for p in head.parameters():
    p.requires_grad = False
reference = Dinov2Model.from_pretrained(BACKBONE).cuda().eval()
for p in reference.parameters():
    p.requires_grad = False

for mode in ('uniform', 'shuffled', 'saliency'):
    path = output / f'{mode}.json'
    if path.exists():
        print(f'skip {path}', flush=True)
        continue
    torch.manual_seed(42)
    model = Dinov2Model.from_pretrained(BACKBONE).cuda()
    count = add_group_lora(model, 'E')
    model.cuda()
    opt = torch.optim.AdamW((p for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad), lr=1e-4, weight_decay=0.01)
    scaler = torch.amp.GradScaler('cuda')
    history = []
    start = time.time()
    for epoch in range(2):
        model.train()
        train_data = DataLoader(FMoWImages(train), batch_size=8, shuffle=True,
                                generator=torch.Generator().manual_seed(42 + epoch),
                                num_workers=2, pin_memory=True)
        ce_values, anchor_values = [], []
        for batch_id, (images, labels, _) in enumerate(tqdm(train_data, desc=f'{mode} epoch {epoch+1}')):
            images, labels = images.cuda(), labels.cuda()
            with torch.no_grad(), torch.autocast('cuda'):
                ref = reference(pixel_values=images).last_hidden_state[:, 1:].float()
                pred = head(ref.mean(1)).argmax(1)
                evidence = (ref * head.weight[pred, None, :]).sum(-1)
                top = evidence.topk(round(0.4 * evidence.shape[1]), dim=1).indices
                weights = torch.zeros_like(evidence)
                weights.scatter_(1, top, 1.0 / top.shape[1])
                if mode == 'uniform':
                    weights.fill_(1.0 / evidence.shape[1])
                elif mode == 'shuffled':
                    random_order = torch.rand(evidence.shape,
                                              generator=torch.Generator().manual_seed(42+epoch*10000+batch_id),
                                              device='cpu').argsort(1).to('cuda')
                    weights = weights.gather(1, random_order)
            with torch.autocast('cuda'):
                tokens = model(pixel_values=images).last_hidden_state[:, 1:].float()
                logits = head(tokens.mean(1))
                ce = nn.functional.cross_entropy(logits, labels)
                anchor = (weights * (tokens - ref).square().mean(-1)).sum(1).mean()
                loss = ce + 0.1 * anchor
            opt.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
            scaler.scale(loss).backward()
            scaler.step(opt)
            scaler.update()
            ce_values.append(ce.item())
            anchor_values.append(anchor.item())
        val_metrics, _ = evaluate(model, head, loader(val, 8, 2, True), 'cuda')
        point = {'epoch': epoch+1, 'cross_entropy': float(np.mean(ce_values)),
                 'anchor_mse': float(np.mean(anchor_values)),
                 'adapt_val_macro_f1': val_metrics['macro_f1']}
        history.append(point)
        print(json.dumps({'mode': mode, **point}), flush=True)
    test_metrics, predictions = evaluate(model, head, loader(test, 8, 2, True), 'cuda')
    counts = np.bincount(predictions, minlength=len(categories))
    result = {'seed': 42, 'mode': mode, 'lambda': 0.1, 'group': 'E',
              'trainable_parameters': count, 'train_count': len(train),
              'val_count': len(val), 'test_count': len(test), 'history': history,
              'future_test': {k:v for k,v in test_metrics.items() if k!='per_class_f1'},
              'unique_predictions': int((counts>0).sum()), 'seconds': time.time()-start}
    path.write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
    print(json.dumps(result), flush=True)
    del model
    torch.cuda.empty_cache()

FILE: scripts/pilots/supervised_check.py
================================================
import json
import time
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np
import torch
from torch import nn
from tqdm import tqdm
from transformers import Dinov2Model

from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_saliency_layer_pilot import divide_groups, evaluate, loader
from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_temporal_pilot import load_manifest
from fyp_drift.evaluation.fmow_layer_selection import add_group_lora
from fyp_drift.models.vision_transformer import BACKBONE

run = Path('/mnt/sdd/FYP/runs/fmow-saliency-layer-full-seed42-20261103')
out = Path('/mnt/sdd/FYP/runs/fmow-supervised-lambda0-check-20261103.json')
if out.exists():
    print(out.read_text())
    raise SystemExit
torch.manual_seed(42)
config = json.loads((run / 'config.json').read_text())
rows, categories = load_manifest(Path(config['manifest']))
adapt_rows = sorted((r for r in rows if r['period'] == 'adapt_2014_15'), key=lambda r: r['local_path'])
future = sorted((r for r in rows if r['period'] == 'future_2016_17'), key=lambda r: r['local_path'])
train, val = divide_groups(adapt_rows, 0.1, 42)
_, test = divide_groups(future, 0.5, 43)
head = nn.Linear(384, len(categories)).cuda().eval()
head.load_state_dict(torch.load(run / 'head.pt', map_location='cuda', weights_only=True))
for p in head.parameters():
    p.requires_grad = False
model = Dinov2Model.from_pretrained(BACKBONE).cuda()
count = add_group_lora(model, 'E')
model.cuda()
optimizer = torch.optim.AdamW((p for p in model.parameters() if p.requires_grad), lr=1e-4, weight_decay=0.01)
scaler = torch.amp.GradScaler('cuda')
history = []
start = time.time()
for epoch in range(2):
    model.train()
    losses, entropies, marginal_entropies = [], [], []
    for images, labels, _ in tqdm(loader(train, 8, 2, True), desc=f'supervised epoch {epoch+1}'):
        with torch.autocast('cuda'):
            tokens = model(pixel_values=images.cuda()).last_hidden_state[:, 1:]
            logits = head(tokens.mean(1).float())
            loss = nn.functional.cross_entropy(logits, labels.cuda())
            prob = logits.softmax(1)
            entropy = -(prob * prob.clamp_min(1e-8).log()).sum(1).mean()
            mean_prob = prob.mean(0)
            marginal = -(mean_prob * mean_prob.clamp_min(1e-8).log()).sum()
        optimizer.zero_grad(set_to_none=True)
        scaler.scale(loss).backward()
        scaler.step(optimizer)
        scaler.update()
        losses.append(loss.item())
        entropies.append(entropy.item())
        marginal_entropies.append(marginal.item())
    metrics, _ = evaluate(model, head, loader(val, 8, 2, True), 'cuda')
    point = {'epoch': epoch+1, 'cross_entropy': float(np.mean(losses)),
             'entropy': float(np.mean(entropies)),
             'marginal_entropy': float(np.mean(marginal_entropies)),
             'adapt_val_macro_f1': metrics['macro_f1']}
    history.append(point)
    print(json.dumps(point), flush=True)
metrics, predictions = evaluate(model, head, loader(test, 8, 2, True), 'cuda')
counts = np.bincount(predictions, minlength=len(categories))
result = {'seed': 42, 'group': 'E', 'lambda': 0, 'trainable_parameters': count,
          'train_count': len(train), 'val_count': len(val), 'test_count': len(test),
          'history': history, 'future_test': {k:v for k,v in metrics.items() if k!='per_class_f1'},
          'unique_predictions': int((counts>0).sum()),
          'top_prediction_classes': np.argsort(-counts)[:10].tolist(),
          'top_prediction_counts': counts[np.argsort(-counts)[:10]].tolist(),
          'seconds': time.time()-start}
out.write_text(json.dumps(result, indent=2))
print(json.dumps(result), flush=True)

    print(json.dumps(point), flush=True)

counts = np.bincount(predictions, minlength=len(categories))
